[codex] # Service-Common - Shared Spring Boot Libraries

[codex] ## Critical Conventions (Always Apply)
[codex] 
[codex] **These rules apply to ALL code changes. No judgment call needed.**
[codex] 
[codex] ### DTO Naming
[codex] - **API request/response classes**: `*Request`, `*Response` (e.g., `CreateTransactionRequest`, `TransactionResponse`)
[codex] - **NEVER use**: `*Dto`, `*DTO`, `*dto` — these suffixes are forbidden
[codex] - **Internal projections**: `*Projection` for JPA projections, `*View` for read models
[codex] 
[codex] ### Variable Naming
[codex] - **Class-level fields**: Use the full class name, not abbreviations
[codex]   - ✅ `private final TransactionRepository transactionRepository;`
[codex]   - ❌ `private final TransactionRepository repository;`
[codex]   - ❌ `private final TransactionRepository txnRepo;`
[codex] - **Local variables**: `var` is preferred, short names acceptable for limited scope
[codex]

[codex] ## Purpose
[codex] 
[codex] Multi-module Gradle project providing shared dependency platforms and runtime libraries for all Budget Analyzer Spring Boot microservices. Publishes four artifacts:
[codex] 
[codex] **spring-platform**: Base backend Spring dependency platform (Spring Boot BOM, Spring Modulith BOM, shared JVM constraints)
[codex] **spring-cloud-platform**: Opt-in Spring Cloud dependency platform overlay
[codex] **service-core**: Minimal-dependency core utilities (base entities, CSV parsing, safe logging)
[codex] **service-web**: Spring Boot web components (exception handling, API error responses, HTTP logging, OpenAPI config)
[codex] 
[codex] **Impacts**: Current consumers are discoverable from the workspace, and changes here affect all services that depend on these libraries.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Module Architecture
[codex] 
[codex] ### spring-platform
[codex] **Location**: `spring-platform/build.gradle.kts`
[codex] 
[codex] **Purpose**: Base Maven BOM-style dependency platform for Budget Analyzer Spring services
[codex] 
[codex] **Contains**:
[codex] - Spring Boot dependency BOM import
[codex] - Spring Modulith dependency BOM import
[codex] - Shared constraints for SpringDoc, Testcontainers, WireMock, Awaitility, and ShedLock
[codex] 
[codex] **Behavior**: Version management only. No source code, runtime dependencies, beans, or autoconfiguration metadata.
[codex] 
[codex] ### spring-cloud-platform
[codex] **Location**: `spring-cloud-platform/build.gradle.kts`
[codex] 
[codex] **Purpose**: Opt-in Maven BOM-style dependency platform for services using Spring Cloud APIs
[codex] 
[codex] **Contains**:
[codex] - spring-platform import
[codex] - Spring Cloud dependency BOM import
[codex] 
[codex] **Behavior**: Version management only. Use only in services that explicitly depend on Spring Cloud libraries.
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-core
[codex] **Location**: `service-core/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/core/`
[codex] 
[codex] **Purpose**: Domain-agnostic utilities with minimal dependencies
[codex] 
[codex] **Contains**:
[codex] - `domain/` - Base JPA entities (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity)
[codex] - `repository/` - Repository utilities (SoftDeleteOperations)
[codex] - `csv/` - CSV parsing (CsvParser, CsvData, OpenCsvParser)
[codex] - `logging/` - Safe logging utilities (`SafeLogger`, `@Sensitive`, `QueryParamSanitizer`, `HttpBodyLogSanitizer`, `SensitiveHeaderMasker`)
[codex] 
[codex] **Dependencies**: Spring Data JPA, Spring Boot Actuator, Jackson, SLF4J, OpenCSV
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-web
[codex] **Location**: `service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/`
[codex] 
[codex] **Purpose**: Spring Boot web components supporting both servlet and reactive stacks
[codex] 
[codex] **Contains**:
[codex] - `api/` - Shared error response models (ApiErrorResponse, ApiErrorType, FieldError, ApiExceptionHandler)
[codex] - `exception/` - Exception hierarchy (shared by both stacks: ResourceNotFoundException, BusinessException, etc.)
[codex] - `config/` - Shared configuration (HttpLoggingProperties, ServiceWebAutoConfiguration, BaseOpenApiConfig)
[codex] - `servlet/` - Servlet-specific implementations (Spring MVC)
[codex]   - `http/` - HTTP filters (CorrelationIdFilter, HttpLoggingFilter, ContentLoggingUtil, HttpLoggingConfig)
[codex]   - `api/` - Exception handler (ServletApiExceptionHandler)
[codex] - `reactive/` - Reactive-specific implementations (Spring WebFlux)
[codex]   - `http/` - Reactive filters (ReactiveCorrelationIdFilter, ReactiveHttpLoggingFilter, body caching decorators, ReactiveHttpLoggingConfig)
[codex]   - `api/` - Exception handler (ReactiveApiExceptionHandler)
[codex] 
[codex] **Dependencies**: service-core (transitive), Spring Boot Starter Web (compileOnly), Spring Boot Starter WebFlux (compileOnly), SpringDoc OpenAPI (compileOnly)
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: service-web supports BOTH servlet and reactive stacks through conditional autoconfiguration.
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: Actuator comes from service-core, available to all services (web and non-web).
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: service-web transitively includes service-core, so consuming services typically only need service-web.
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View platform build definitions
[codex] cat spring-platform/build.gradle.kts
[codex] cat spring-cloud-platform/build.gradle.kts
[codex] 
[codex] # View published modules configured for this build
[codex] cat settings.gradle.kts
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When to Use These Libraries
[codex] 
[codex] - ✅ Cross-service utilities (logging, error handling, common DTOs)
[codex] - ✅ Shared Spring Boot configurations
[codex] - ✅ Common dependencies and version management
[codex] - ❌ Service-specific business logic (belongs in service repos)
[codex] 
[codex] ### Which Module to Depend On?
[codex] 
[codex] **Servlet service with database (most common):**
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-web")
[codex]     implementation("org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-data-jpa")
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Reactive service (e.g., Session Gateway):**
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-webflux")
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Spring Cloud service:**
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-cloud-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-starter-web")
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Non-web service (batch jobs, workers):**
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-core:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Use the checked-in version literal from `build.gradle.kts` when consuming the
[codex] published artifacts.
[codex] 
[codex] **Why explicit dependencies?** Web stack and JPA are `compileOnly` in service-common to prevent classpath conflicts (reactive inheriting servlet) and unnecessary transitive dependencies (reactive services inheriting JPA). Platform artifacts manage versions only; consuming services must still declare the libraries they use.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Spring Boot Conventions
[codex] 
[codex] **THE canonical source of truth** for Spring Boot patterns across all Budget Analyzer microservices.
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Clean layered architecture (Controller → Service → Repository) with standardized naming, pure JPA persistence, and base entity classes for common functionality.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to consult details**:
[codex] - Setting up new service → Read [Architecture Layers](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md#architecture-layers) and [Package Structure](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md#package-structure)
[codex] - Creating entities → Read [Base Entity Classes](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md#base-entity-classes) (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity)
[codex] - Writing controllers → Read [HTTP Response Patterns](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md#http-response-patterns) (201 Created with Location header)
[codex] - Dependency injection → Read [Dependency Injection](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md#dependency-injection) section
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference**:
[codex] - Controllers: `*Controller` + `@RestController` + thin (HTTP concerns only)
[codex] - Services: `*Service` concrete classes + `@Service` + `@Transactional` at this layer
[codex] - Repositories: `*Repository` extends `JpaRepository&lt;Entity, ID>`
[codex] - Providers: `*Provider` interface + implementation (for external service boundaries only)
[codex] - Pure JPA only: **Forbidden** `org.hibernate.*` → **Use** `jakarta.persistence.*`
[codex] - Base entities: Extend `AuditableEntity` (timestamps) or `SoftDeletableEntity` (soft delete)
[codex] 
[codex] **For comprehensive patterns, read [docs/spring-boot-conventions.md](docs/spring-boot-conventions.md) when working on architecture tasks.**
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View service-core package structure
[codex] find service-core/src/main/java -type d | grep -E "org/budgetanalyzer"
[codex] 
[codex] # View service-web package structure
[codex] find service-web/src/main/java -type d | grep -E "org/budgetanalyzer"
[codex] 
[codex] # Find all base entities (in service-core)
[codex] grep -r "@MappedSuperclass" service-core/src/
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Exception Handling
[codex] 
[codex] **Module**: service-web
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Centralized `@RestControllerAdvice` handler that converts all exceptions to standardized `ApiErrorResponse` format with HTTP status codes, error types, and field-level validation messages.
[codex]

[codex] # Dependency Automation Plan
[codex] 
[codex] **Status:** Proposed; automation is not installed or enabled by this document.
[codex] 
[codex] Use Renovate to discover dependency updates and open reviewable pull requests across
[codex] Budget Analyzer. Keep GitHub Dependabot alerts enabled for vulnerability detection,
[codex] and use existing open-source scanners for dependencies that update discovery alone
[codex] cannot assess. No dependency upgrades are part of implementing this plan.
[codex] 
[codex] Preserve [the September 6 review](../research/dependency-update-review-2026-09-06.md)
[codex] unchanged as the acceptance benchmark. Its versions are historical observations,
[codex] not inputs to the bot's target selection. Automation must independently discover
[codex] equivalent or newer applicable updates. Record misses explicitly; an update PR is
[codex] not proof that the associated security advisory was detected.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Selected tools and cost
[codex] 
[codex] | Purpose | Selection | Cost and boundary |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Version and digest update PRs | Open-source Renovate, run by the Mend Renovate Community GitHub App | The app explicitly supports public and private repositories without a paid plan. No self-hosted bot infrastructure is needed. |
[codex] | Dependency vulnerability alerts | GitHub dependency graph and Dependabot alerts | Available on GitHub Free. GitHub's hosted service is proprietary; the update engine and added scanning tools are open source. |
[codex] | Resolved Java dependency graph | Official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` | Use the open-source `cache-provider: basic` path, not the proprietary enhanced cache or a preview subscription. |
[codex] | Image packages and vulnerabilities | Trivy CLI/action | Open source; no Aqua subscription, server, or commercial database required. |
[codex] | Frontend and Go verification | `npm audit` and `govulncheck` | Existing ecosystem tools; no subscription. |
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate is the sole update-PR owner. Do not add Dependabot version-update
[codex] configuration or enable overlapping Dependabot security-update PRs. Renovate can
[codex] consume Dependabot alerts and attempt security fixes itself. Alert-only findings,
[codex] especially inherited Gradle versions, remain visible when it cannot produce a fix.
[codex] 
[codex] The free Mend service currently documents unlimited repositories, one concurrent
[codex] job per organization, approximately four-hour scheduling for active repositories,
[codex] and a 30-minute job timeout. That is adequate for initial dependency discovery;
[codex] the pilot must verify actual completion for this project. Paid Merge Confidence,
[codex] enterprise scheduling, support, and approval for enhanced OSS resources are not
[codex] prerequisites.
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Actions compute and artifact storage are a separate cost consideration.
[codex] Use standard Linux runners, short artifact retention, and existing jobs where
[codex] practical. Public-repository standard runners are free; private-repository usage
[codex] must fit the account's included allowance with paid overages disabled. Repository
[codex] visibility, billing settings, and bot installation cannot be inferred from the
[codex] checkout; the unauthenticated API check during planning returned HTTP 403.
[codex] Before activation, verify these settings. If a necessary capability requires a
[codex] subscription, trial conversion, paid runner, or additional spend, stop and discuss
[codex] it with the user. Do not silently switch to a paid offering or provision a server.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Scope and operating policy
[codex] 
[codex] The rollout covers `orchestration`, `service-common`, `currency-service`,
[codex] `permission-service`, `transaction-service`, `session-gateway`,
[codex] `budget-analyzer-web`, `ext-authz`, `workspace`, and
[codex] `budget-analyzer-api-tests`. Other sibling checkouts are outside this rollout.
[codex] Each sibling phase executes in that repository's context and reads its own
[codex] `AGENTS.md`; orchestration workers must not implement sibling service logic.
[codex] 
[codex] The implementation consists of repository configuration, small CI additions, and
[codex] documentation. Use native Renovate managers first. For existing shell variables,
[codex] Tilt strings, and download URLs, use Renovate's documented declarative regex
[codex] manager and annotation comments. This is configuration of an existing tool, not a
[codex] new dependency crawler. Do not build custom scanners, advisory matching code,
[codex] version-selection scripts, lifecycle services, dashboards, or cross-repo bots.
[codex] 
[codex] Put common rules in `renovate-presets/default.json` in orchestration. Sibling
[codex] `renovate.json` files extend
[codex] `github>budgetanalyzer/orchestration//renovate-presets/default`; keep file-specific
[codex] extraction rules in their owning repository. Publish the preset before activating
[codex] consumers. Documentation belongs in a future `docs/dependency-automation.md`,
[codex] with sibling docs linking to it instead of copying detailed policy.
[codex] 
[codex] Initial policy:
[codex] 
[codex] - Start from `config:recommended`, enable the Dependency Dashboard, and set
[codex]   `automerge: false` everywhere, including security fixes.
[codex] - Create routine PRs weekly, initially at most three open routine PRs per repo
[codex]   and two new routine PRs per hour. Security fixes bypass the routine schedule;
[codex]   verify the effective Renovate configuration preserves that behavior.
[codex] - Keep patch, minor, and major recommendations distinguishable. Show both
[codex]   maintained-line updates and later release lines where supported by the
[codex]   datasource. Use dashboard approval for majors and stateful/platform migrations;
[codex]   do not hide them using blanket major exclusions or restrictive version ceilings.
[codex] - Group only concrete compatibility families: React/React DOM, Vitest packages,
[codex]   related Spring coordinates within a repo, repeated Istio pins, and related
[codex]   chart/CLI declarations. Helm chart majors are not application majors: all
[codex]   controller/chart updates need review even when semver calls them a patch.
[codex] - Keep Helm 4, RabbitMQ/PostgreSQL migrations, and Kubernetes/Istio coordination
[codex]   visible as proposals requiring compatibility review. Bots cannot coordinate an
[codex]   atomic merge across repositories or infer the correct Spring release train.
[codex] - Enable digest refreshes and retain digest pinning and image flavor suffixes.
[codex]   Never replace an immutable reference with a floating tag. Verify ARM64 support
[codex]   before accepting image updates; an available registry tag is insufficient.
[codex] - Exclude historical docs, generated output, local `:tilt-*` images, and documented
[codex]   local image repositories from updates. First-party production artifact promotion
[codex]   remains owned by the existing release workflow and its synchronized manifest,
[codex]   inventory, and overlay; Renovate must not update those independently.
[codex] - New checks report the existing vulnerability backlog without blocking every
[codex]   unrelated PR. Publish findings and distinguish scan failures from findings;
[codex]   do not swallow authentication, database-download, or dependency-resolution
[codex]   failures. Introducing a vulnerability merge gate is a separate decision.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Coverage contract
[codex] 
[codex] Create an ordinary Markdown coverage report during implementation at
[codex] `docs/research/dependency-automation-coverage.md`. It records source paths,
[codex] extracted package identities, observed bot/scanner outputs, and remaining gaps.
[codex] Do not maintain a second hand-curated table of desired dependency versions.
[codex] 
[codex] | Baseline area | Automated update discovery | Security/support proof and expected limits |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Boot, Cloud, Modulith, SpringDoc, Testcontainers, other Java dependencies | Renovate Gradle/version-catalog and wrapper managers in all five Java repos | Submit resolved graphs, including Framework, Security, Jackson, Tomcat and Netty, to Dependabot. A Boot PR alone does not establish that its BOM fixes every finding. Overrides require service-owner assessment. |
[codex] | Frontend direct and transitive packages; later toolchain majors | Renovate npm manager, lockfile maintenance, Dockerfile and Actions managers | Dependabot alerts plus full and production-only `npm audit`. Compare advisory/package identities, not the old total of 20 findings. Preserve browser/SSR/dev-server applicability distinctions. |
[codex] | go-redis and Go runtime | Renovate gomod, Dockerfile, Actions, and workspace download extraction | `govulncheck ./...` must reproduce or explain GO-2025-3540 and distinguish reachable calls from package presence. |
[codex] | Redis, PostgreSQL, RabbitMQ, NGINX, Temurin, Jaeger, Swagger UI and other deployed third-party images | Dockerfile/Kubernetes/Helm-values managers plus Tilt and script extraction; digest updates | Trivy scans the exact selected digest and inventories the embedded software. A new digest does not prove the exact Redis or JDK patch or advisory fix. Missing package identification is a coverage gap. |
[codex] | External Secrets, Kyverno, cert-manager, Istio, kube-prometheus-stack, Kiali | Helm datasource mapped to shell and Tilt pins; explicit chart names and registry URLs | Render selected charts and scan their actual image refs. Chart versions differ from app versions. Controller advisories may lack matching image-package metadata and must be recorded as misses. |
[codex] | Prometheus, Grafana, Prometheus Operator, kube-state-metrics | Helm-values image refs plus chart updates | Scan explicitly overridden images as well as chart defaults. Updating a chart does not update an independently overridden image tag. |
[codex] | K3s, Kind binary/node image, Kubernetes tooling, Gateway API, Calico, Helm, Tilt and other pinned tools | Built-in Docker/release datasources and annotated existing pins | Show supported-line patches and newer releases independently. Tool versions coupled to checked-in checksums need complete checksum changes before merge. Pod Security labels are compatibility settings, not ordinary package pins. |
[codex] | Workspace base image, Node/Go, Zulu and installed packages | Dockerfile/ARG/download extraction where versions are declared | Scan a built workspace image for installed versions. Unversioned apt installs receive updates through rebuilds; Renovate cannot open a version PR for an absent patch pin. Preserve the digest-only Ubuntu base constraint. |
[codex] | GitHub Actions and Python API-test dependencies | Native Actions and Python managers where constraints/lockfiles permit updates | Unbounded Python requirements are not version pins. Report their detection and installed-environment audit coverage without pretending an unbounded spec can receive routine version bumps. |
[codex] | EOL decisions: Boot, Node, Go, RabbitMQ, NGINX, Kubernetes/K3s and Istio | Newer releases should be visible in the bot dashboard | Renovate release discovery, abandonment heuristics, and vulnerability scanners are not a comprehensive EOL detector. Retain a quarterly human support-policy check against upstream lifecycle sources. |
[codex] 
[codex] Both patch and major proposals must remain visible for broad tags such as
[codex] `redis:7-alpine` and `eclipse-temurin:25-jre-alpine`. Test actual extraction and
[codex] lookup: broad tags can produce only digest updates and conceal patch identities.
[codex] If a same-digest, explicit-version alias is needed for reliable recommendations,
[codex] record that as a separately reviewed representation change; do not silently
[codex] upgrade the underlying image during onboarding.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep tool versions with checksum tables, chart/application coupling, and the
[codex] digest-only workspace base behind dashboard approval until a proposal contains
[codex] all required companion changes. Use existing Renovate checksum capabilities where
[codex] they fit and verify them; regex extraction alone does not calculate arbitrary
[codex] checksums. Otherwise identify the missing companion work in the proposal and
[codex] retain existing validation failures. Do not weaken verification to make a bot PR
[codex] mergeable.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Acceptance and activation boundaries
[codex] 
[codex] The target is automated coverage of the same or a superset of the review's update
[codex] and security findings. Measure update discovery, advisory detection, and lifecycle
[codex] assessment separately. For every baseline item, record one of: reproduced,
[codex] superseded by a newer applicable finding, false positive with evidence, or missing.
[codex] Include the advisory identifier, affected package/version, proposed target where
[codex] available, output URL/artifact, and any required human compatibility decision.
[codex] 
[codex] All baseline areas must have a demonstrated extraction/scan path. Missing
[codex] high-priority findings block a claim of full review parity. Tool installation can
[codex] be reported complete while parity remains incomplete, but those are distinct
[codex] outcomes. If standard configuration and the selected tools cannot close a gap,
[codex] report it to the user; do not fabricate an advisory match, seed expected versions
[codex] into configuration, or add a custom implementation. The lifecycle and
[codex] exploitability limits above remain explicit even after successful onboarding.
[codex] 
[codex] Implementation phases prepare reviewable local files. No phase authorizes git
[codex] writes, merging dependency PRs, releases, certificate writes, or cluster changes.
[codex] The user owns committing and publishing changes. Before final observation, a
[codex] repository administrator installs the free Renovate App only on the scoped repos,
[codex] enables dependency graph and Dependabot alerts, grants the app alert-read access,
[codex] and confirms duplicate update-PR services are off. Prepare all configuration and
[codex] documentation before this activation handoff. Do not change GitHub settings while
[codex] merely executing this planning request.
[codex] 
[codex] Existing Maven package credentials are a prerequisite for complete Java graph
[codex] resolution and successful bot PR CI. Use the existing scoped GitHub Packages read
[codex] credentials in trusted CI, and Mend's supported encrypted credential/host-rule
[codex] configuration if Renovate needs private Maven lookup. Never put plaintext tokens
[codex] in presets or reports. Verify the free app supports the required authenticated
[codex] lookup before activation; do not replace it with Maven Local or drop internal
[codex] dependencies from the graph. Do not introduce privileged `pull_request_target`
[codex] execution of dependency branches to obtain secrets.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Execution runtime prerequisite
[codex] 
[codex] Before resuming this plan at Phase 2, the user will upgrade the sibling
[codex] `../workspace` sandbox from Node 22 to Node 24, update that repository's nearest
[codex] runtime documentation, rebuild the development container, and complete the
[codex] workspace-owned post-rebuild checks. This is a user-owned prerequisite, not a
[codex] dependency upgrade implemented by this plan. An orchestration worker must not edit
[codex] the workspace repository to satisfy it.
[codex] 
[codex] At the start of the next runner invocation, verify `node --version` reports major
[codex] version 24 and that `npm --version` succeeds. Stop if the rebuilt runtime is not
[codex] active. Renovate `44.65.5` previously loaded under Node `22.23.2` emitted an
[codex] unhandled `RegExp.escape is not a function` rejection while returning exit status
[codex] zero. Treat any uncaught exception, unhandled rejection, or fatal/error log from
[codex] Renovate as validation failure regardless of process status. After this prerequisite
[codex] is complete, run local Renovate validation directly under the workspace's Node 24;
[codex] do not hide a stale container behind an ad hoc downloaded Node runtime.
[codex] 
[codex] The manual Node 22-to-24 change becomes the declared baseline for later extraction
[codex] and image scanning. Record it as a prerequisite change, not as a Renovate-discovered
[codex] or bot-applied finding when comparing automation with the saved review.
[codex]

[codex] ## Testing Patterns
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Test pure logic with real objects and application behavior with real Spring beans. Use
[codex] Testcontainers for persistence and brokers, and WireMock for external HTTP.
[codex] 
[codex] **Rule**: Do not mock or spy application-owned Spring beans. Before writing or modifying tests,
[codex] read [docs/testing-patterns.md](docs/testing-patterns.md) and follow its guidance for real
[codex] components and concrete test implementations.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to consult details**:
[codex] - Writing unit tests → Read [Unit Testing Patterns](docs/testing-patterns.md#unit-tests) (real objects, naming, structure)
[codex] - Writing integration tests → Read [Integration Testing](docs/testing-patterns.md#integration-tests) (Testcontainers, @SpringBootTest)
[codex] - Setting up Testcontainers → Read [Testcontainers Setup](docs/testing-patterns.md#testcontainers) (PostgreSQL, Redis, RabbitMQ)
[codex] - Understanding test philosophy → Read [Testing Philosophy](docs/testing-patterns.md#testing-philosophy) (test correct behavior)
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference**:
[codex] - **Assertions**: AssertJ exclusively (`assertThat`). Do NOT use JUnit assertions (`assertEquals`, `assertTrue`, `assertThrows`, etc.)
[codex] - Unit tests: `*Test.java` (no Spring context, fast, isolated)
[codex] - Integration tests: `*IntegrationTest.java` (with `@SpringBootTest` + Testcontainers)
[codex] - Minimum coverage: 80% line coverage via JaCoCo
[codex] - Test correct behavior: Fix bugs, don't write tests around defective implementations
[codex] - Testcontainers: Auto-starts PostgreSQL/Redis/RabbitMQ in Docker for integration tests
[codex] 
[codex] **For comprehensive testing strategies, read [docs/testing-patterns.md](docs/testing-patterns.md) when writing tests.**
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View test structure in service-core
[codex] find service-core/src/test/java -name "*Test.java"
[codex] 
[codex] # View test structure in service-web
[codex] find service-web/src/test/java -name "*Test.java"
[codex] 
[codex] # Run tests with coverage (all modules)
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Key Features
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Shared utilities and base classes for cross-cutting concerns.
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference by module** (current entry points; verify with discovery before assuming completeness):
[codex] 
[codex] **service-core**:
[codex] - Base Entities: `AuditableEntity` (timestamps + user tracking), `SoftDeletableEntity` (soft delete)
[codex] - CSV Parsing: `CsvParser`, `CsvData`, `OpenCsvParser`
[codex] - Safe Logging: `SafeLogger` (opt-in: `toJson()`, `mask()`, `truncateId()`), `@Sensitive` annotation, `QueryParamSanitizer`, `HttpBodyLogSanitizer`, `SensitiveHeaderMasker`
[codex] - Repository: `SoftDeleteOperations`
[codex] - User Tracking: `SecurityContextAuditorAware` (auto-populates createdBy/updatedBy from Security context)
[codex] 
[codex] **service-web**:
[codex] - Exceptions: `ResourceNotFoundException`, `BusinessException`, `InvalidRequestException`, `ServiceException`
[codex] - API Models: `ApiErrorResponse`, `ApiErrorType`, `FieldError`
[codex] - HTTP Filters: `CorrelationIdFilter`, `HttpLoggingFilter`
[codex] - Configuration: `BaseOpenApiConfig`, `HttpLoggingConfig`
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View base entities (service-core)

[codex]

[codex]

[codex]

[codex] find service-web/src/main/java -name "*.java"
[codex] ```

[codex] ## Publishing and Consumption
[codex] 
[codex] Use `./gradlew publishToMavenLocal` for local development publishing.
[codex] 
[codex] Use the checked-in version literal from `build.gradle.kts` when consuming the
[codex] published artifacts.
[codex] 
[codex] Import `spring-platform` in non-Cloud Spring services. Import
[codex] `spring-cloud-platform` instead for services that use Spring Cloud APIs; it
[codex] already imports `spring-platform`.
[codex] 
[codex] Use `service-web` by default; depend on `service-core` directly only when you
[codex] need the minimal non-web module.
[codex] 
[codex] Published coordinates:
[codex] ```text
[codex] org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>
[codex] org.budgetanalyzer:spring-cloud-platform:&lt;service-common-version>
[codex] org.budgetanalyzer:service-core:&lt;service-common-version>
[codex] org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Local development still stays on `publishToMavenLocal`; GitHub Packages is for
[codex] CI, release, and intentional isolated-build scenarios.
[codex] 
[codex] Remote publish, release/version rules, and published coordinates live in
[codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md).

[codex] ## Autoconfiguration
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Both modules use Spring Boot autoconfiguration mechanism - consuming services get most functionality automatically without manual configuration or component scanning.
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View autoconfiguration files
[codex] cat service-core/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] cat service-web/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] 
[codex] # See what's registered
[codex] grep -r "@Configuration\|@AutoConfiguration" service-*/src/main/java/
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-core: Automatic Configuration
[codex] 
[codex] **Registered via**: `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`
[codex] 
[codex] #### What You Get Automatically
[codex] 
[codex] **ServiceCoreAutoConfiguration** - Activated when service-core is on classpath:
[codex] - **JPA Entity Scanning** (`@EntityScan`) - Automatically scans `org.budgetanalyzer.core.domain` for base entities (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity)
[codex]   - Conditional: Only when `DataSource` exists
[codex] - **JPA Auditing** (`@EnableJpaAuditing`) - Enables automatic timestamps and user tracking on entities extending AuditableEntity
[codex]   - Conditional: Only when `DataSource` exists
[codex]   - Provides `createdAt`, `updatedAt` (timestamps) and `createdBy`, `updatedBy` (user tracking)
[codex] - **SecurityContextAuditorAware Bean** - Provides current user ID for JPA auditing
[codex]   - Extracts user ID from `Authentication.getName()` in Spring Security context
[codex]   - Contains internal user ID (e.g., `usr_507f1f77bcf86cd799439011`) from `X-User-Id` header
[codex]   - Returns empty for anonymous users or system operations (fields remain null)
[codex] - **OpenCsvParser Bean** - CSV parser component automatically available for injection
[codex] 
[codex] #### What Requires Manual Use
[codex] 
[codex] These are utilities (not Spring beans) - instantiate manually when needed:
[codex] - `SafeLogger` - **Opt-in** static utility for safe logging (does NOT intercept log calls automatically):
[codex]   - `SafeLogger.toJson(obj)` — serialize with `@Sensitive` field masking
[codex]   - `SafeLogger.mask(value)` / `SafeLogger.mask(value, showLast)` — mask sensitive strings
[codex]   - `SafeLogger.truncateId(id)` / `SafeLogger.truncateId(id, showChars)` — truncate identifiers for logs (e.g. `550e8400…`)
[codex] - `SensitiveDataModule` - Jackson module for `@Sensitive` annotation
[codex] - Base entity classes (`AuditableEntity`, `SoftDeletableEntity`) - Extend in your entities
[codex] - `SoftDeleteOperations` - Repository utility interface
[codex] 
[codex] **Discovery**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View ServiceCoreAutoConfiguration
[codex] cat service-core/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/core/config/ServiceCoreAutoConfiguration.java
[codex] 
[codex] # Find all @Component beans in service-core
[codex] grep -r "@Component" service-core/src/main/java/
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-web: Automatic Configuration
[codex] 
[codex] **Registered via**: `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`
[codex] 
[codex] #### What You Get Automatically
[codex] 
[codex] **ServiceWebAutoConfiguration** - Conditionally registers servlet OR reactive components based on classpath:
[codex] 
[codex] **For Servlet Applications** (Spring MVC):
[codex] - Activates when `@ConditionalOnWebApplication(type = SERVLET)`
[codex] - **ServletApiExceptionHandler** - Global exception handling with `@RestControllerAdvice`
[codex]   - Converts all exceptions to standardized `ApiErrorResponse` format
[codex]   - Handles: `InvalidRequestException` (400), `AuthenticationException` (401), `AccessDeniedException` (403), `AuthorizationDeniedException` (403), `ResourceNotFoundException` (404), `BusinessException` (422), `ServiceException` (500), validation errors, generic exceptions
[codex]   - Priority: `@Order(LOWEST_PRECEDENCE)` - services can override
[codex] - **CorrelationIdFilter** - Always enabled - Adds correlation ID (MDC + response header)
[codex] - **HttpLoggingFilter** - Opt-in via configuration
[codex]   - Redacts common secret fields in JSON and form bodies
[codex]   - Omits multipart, binary, and compressed bodies with placeholders
[codex] - **HttpLoggingConfig** - Configuration for servlet filters
[codex] 
[codex] **For Reactive Applications** (Spring WebFlux):
[codex] - Activates when `@ConditionalOnWebApplication(type = REACTIVE)`
[codex] - **ReactiveApiExceptionHandler** - Global exception handling with `@ControllerAdvice`
[codex]   - Converts all exceptions to standardized `ApiErrorResponse` format
[codex]   - Handles: `InvalidRequestException` (400), `AuthenticationException` (401), `AccessDeniedException` (403), `AuthorizationDeniedException` (403), `ResourceNotFoundException` (404), `BusinessException` (422), reactive-specific validation (`WebExchangeBindException`), generic exceptions
[codex]   - Returns `Mono&lt;ResponseEntity&lt;ApiErrorResponse>>`
[codex] - **ReactiveCorrelationIdFilter** - Always enabled - Adds correlation ID (Reactor Context + response header)
[codex] - **ReactiveHttpLoggingFilter** - Opt-in via configuration
[codex]   - Redacts common secret fields in JSON and form bodies
[codex]   - Omits multipart, binary, and compressed bodies with placeholders
[codex] - **ReactiveHttpLoggingConfig** - Configuration for reactive filters
[codex] 
[codex] **Shared Configuration**:
[codex] - **HttpLoggingProperties** - Shared between servlet and reactive
[codex] - **ClaimsHeaderSecurityConfig** / **ReactiveClaimsHeaderSecurityConfig** - Header-based security using trusted ingress external-auth claims
[codex]   - Reads `X-User-Id`, `X-Permissions`, `X-Roles` headers injected by the trusted ingress auth path
[codex]   - Extracts permissions as direct authorities (e.g., `transactions:read`)
[codex]   - Extracts roles as ROLE_-prefixed authorities (e.g., `ROLE_ADMIN`)
[codex]   - Enables method security for both stacks, including reactive `@PreAuthorize` enforcement in WebFlux services
[codex]   - Public endpoints: `/actuator/health/**`, `/actuator/prometheus/**`, OpenAPI docs
[codex]   - Protected: All other endpoints (requires `X-User-Id` header)
[codex]   - Stateless by default; `/internal/**` is not globally anonymous
[codex]   - The shared default filter chain backs off when a service defines its own `SecurityFilterChain` or `SecurityWebFilterChain`
[codex]   - No external properties needed (no JWKS URI, no issuer config)
[codex] 
[codex] **Enable HTTP logging** (optional, works for both stacks):
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] budgetanalyzer:
[codex]   service:
[codex]     http-logging:
[codex]       enabled: true
[codex]       include-query-params: false  # Safer default; opt in only when query strings are known-safe
[codex]       include-client-ip: true
[codex]       include-request-headers: true
[codex]       include-response-headers: true
[codex]       include-request-body: true   # Optional: defaults to false; JSON/form secrets are redacted
[codex]       include-response-body: true  # Optional: defaults to false; binary/multipart/compressed bodies are omitted
[codex]       max-body-size: 10000         # Optional: values &lt;= 0 disable body capture
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **OpenAPI Base Configuration** (`BaseOpenApiConfig`):
[codex] - Abstract base class for service-specific OpenAPI configuration
[codex] - Automatically provides: Standard error response schemas (400/404/500/503)
[codex] - **Requires manual setup**: Service must extend `BaseOpenApiConfig`
[codex]   ```java
[codex]   @Configuration
[codex]   @OpenApiDefinition(
[codex]       info = @Info(title = "Your Service API", version = "1.0.0")
[codex]   )
[codex]   public class OpenApiConfig extends BaseOpenApiConfig {}
[codex]   ```

[codex] - **SecurityContextUtil** - Static utility for extracting user info from Security context
[codex] - **ContentLoggingUtil** - Utility for HTTP content logging
[codex] - **Exception classes** - Throw these in your code: `ResourceNotFoundException`, `InvalidRequestException`, `BusinessException`, `ServiceException`, `ClientException`, `ServiceUnavailableException`
[codex] - **API response models** - DTOs for error responses: `ApiErrorResponse`, `ApiErrorType`, `FieldError`
[codex] - **Test utilities** - `TestClaimsSecurityConfig`, `ClaimsHeaderTestBuilder` (for tests)

[codex] ### Summary: Action Required by Consuming Services
[codex] 
[codex] **service-core** (fully automatic):
[codex] - ✅ Nothing - works automatically when on classpath
[codex] - ✅ Base entities, auditing, CSV parser all autoconfigured
[codex] 
[codex] **service-web** (mostly automatic):
[codex] - ✅ Exception handling - automatic
[codex] - ✅ Security - automatic by default (no properties needed; trusts claims headers from the ingress external-auth path)
[codex] - ✅ Consumer-defined Spring Security chains override the shared default chain on both servlet and reactive stacks
[codex] - ✅ Correlation ID filter - automatic
[codex] - ⚙️ HTTP logging - opt-in via `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.enabled=true`; structured text bodies are redacted and unsafe body types are omitted
[codex] - ⚙️ OpenAPI - extend `BaseOpenApiConfig` with `@Configuration` + `@OpenApiDefinition`
[codex] 
[codex] **IMPORTANT**: Component scanning of `org.budgetanalyzer.service` is **NOT required** for autoconfiguration. The Spring Boot autoconfiguration mechanism handles everything automatically via `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Architectural Principles
[codex] 
[codex] ### Architectural Simplicity (KISS)
[codex] 
[codex] **Primary rule: Keep it simple.** Choose the simplest implementation that correctly handles realistic inputs, states, and failure modes. Simplicity must not come at the expense of security, data integrity, or required behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] - Put validation in the layer that owns the rule: request models and controllers validate request shape and syntax; services validate business invariants, ownership, persistence state, and cross-entity rules.
[codex] - Do not duplicate API validation in the service layer when every call reaches the service through the validated API contract. Add service-level validation when another caller can bypass that contract or when the service owns the rule.
[codex] - Do not add a guard, fallback, or custom exception path for a state made impossible by an enforced boundary or invariant.
[codex] - At external or asynchronous boundaries, handle plausible failures explicitly because they are outside the local code's control.
[codex] - Before adding a defensive branch, identify how the state can arise and what the caller or system can usefully do in response. If neither is concrete, omit the branch.
[codex] - Prefer a direct implementation and established project patterns over speculative abstractions or extension points.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Production-Quality Code
[codex] All code must be production-ready. No shortcuts, prototypes, or workarounds.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to read more**: [docs/common-patterns.md](docs/common-patterns.md) - SOLID principles, design patterns, Spring Boot patterns, database patterns, security/performance best practices.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Pure JPA Only
[codex] **CRITICAL**: Use pure JPA (Jakarta Persistence API) exclusively. NO Hibernate-specific features.
[codex] - Forbidden: `org.hibernate.*`
[codex] - Allowed: `jakarta.persistence.*`
[codex] 
[codex] ### Vendor Independence
[codex] **CRITICAL**: Avoid lock-in to external providers. Abstract all external dependencies behind interfaces. Internal identifiers must be provider-agnostic.
[codex] 
[codex] **Applies to**: Auth providers (e.g., Auth0, Okta), data providers (FRED), cloud services (AWS/GCP), payment processors, email services, SMS gateways — any external system.
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Service → Interface → Provider Implementation → External Client. The interface is yours; the implementation is swappable.
[codex] 
[codex] **Identifiers**: Use prefixed string IDs (`usr_`, `txn_`, `req_`) as foreign keys and in cross-service communication. These IDs flow across service boundaries and into JWTs — auto-increment longs couple identity to a single database; string UUIDs are portable by default. Prefixes make IDs self-describing in logs, JWTs, and database queries. Always use full UUIDs (32 hex chars) — never truncate (entropy reduction with no storage benefit since the type is `String` either way). Format: `{prefix}_{full-uuid-hex}` (e.g., `usr_507f1f77bcf86cd799439011abcdef12`). Translate external IDs (IdP sub, Stripe customer ID) at the boundary. Never let external ID formats leak into your domain model.
[codex] 
[codex] **Why**: The cost of abstraction is bounded (one interface, one translation layer). The cost of provider migration without abstraction is unbounded (every table, every service, every log).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Standardized Error Responses
[codex] All API errors follow `ApiErrorResponse` format with error types, field-level validation, and error codes.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Soft Delete Pattern
[codex] Entities extending `SoftDeletableEntity` are never actually deleted from the database - only marked as deleted.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Backwards Compatibility: CI/CD-Driven Lockstep Upgrades
[codex] **CRITICAL**: ALL changes to spring-platform, spring-cloud-platform, service-core, and service-web MUST be backwards compatible. We maintain a common platform across all microservices and upgrade all services in lockstep when we upgrade these artifacts.
[codex] 
[codex] All four artifacts are versioned and released together as a coordinated set.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to consult details**:
[codex] - Determining if a change is breaking → Read [What's Breaking vs. Safe](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#examples-whats-breaking-vs-safe)
[codex] - Deprecating APIs → Read [Deprecation Process](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#3-deprecate-before-removal)
[codex] - Database schema changes → Read [Database Migrations](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#database-migrations)
[codex] - Version bumps → Read [Semantic Versioning](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#semantic-versioning) and [Version Bump Checklist](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#version-bump-checklist)
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference**:
[codex] - Add new, don't modify existing (extend rather than change)
[codex] - Platform changes can be breaking when they remove constraints or move major framework versions
[codex] - Prefer expand/contract database migrations; incompatible schema or data changes cannot rely on default Kubernetes rolling updates
[codex] - Test against all consuming services before release
[codex] - Major version bumps require coordinated migration across all services
[codex] 
[codex] **For comprehensive compatibility guidelines, read [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md) when making any changes to service-common artifacts.**
[codex] 
[codex] ### Code Quality Standards
[codex] - **Spotless**: Google Java Format
[codex] - **Checkstyle**: Custom rules (including Hibernate import ban)
[codex] - **Variable declarations**: Use `var` whenever possible
[codex] - **Variable naming**: Class-level fields MUST use full class name (e.g., `currencySeriesRepository` not `repository`)
[codex] - **Imports**: No wildcard imports, always explicit
[codex] - **Javadoc**: First sentence MUST end with period (`.`)
[codex] 
[codex] **When to read more**: [docs/code-quality-standards.md](docs/code-quality-standards.md) - Complete Spotless/Checkstyle configuration, detailed formatting rules, IDE setup, troubleshooting.
[codex] 
[codex] **Build commands**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Always run these two commands in sequence

[codex] /workspace/orchestration/docs/decisions/004-service-common-dependency-strategy.md:9:The Budget Analyzer project has a shared library (`service-common`) that provides common utilities, configurations, and patterns for all microservices. As the project evolves, we need to support different types of applications:

[codex] #### What Requires Manual Use
[codex] 
[codex] These are utilities (not Spring beans) or exception classes for throwing:
[codex] - **SecurityContextUtil** - Static utility for extracting user info from Security context
[codex] - **ContentLoggingUtil** - Utility for HTTP content logging
[codex] - **Exception classes** - Throw these in your code: `ResourceNotFoundException`, `InvalidRequestException`, `BusinessException`, `ServiceException`, `ClientException`, `ServiceUnavailableException`
[codex] - **API response models** - DTOs for error responses: `ApiErrorResponse`, `ApiErrorType`, `FieldError`
[codex] - **Test utilities** - `TestClaimsSecurityConfig`, `ClaimsHeaderTestBuilder` (for tests)
[codex]

[codex] ### Summary: Action Required by Consuming Services
[codex] 
[codex] **service-core** (fully automatic):
[codex] - ✅ Nothing - works automatically when on classpath
[codex] - ✅ Base entities, auditing, CSV parser all autoconfigured
[codex] 
[codex] **service-web** (mostly automatic):
[codex] - ✅ Exception handling - automatic
[codex] - ✅ Security - automatic by default (no properties needed; trusts claims headers from the ingress external-auth path)
[codex] - ✅ Consumer-defined Spring Security chains override the shared default chain on both servlet and reactive stacks
[codex] - ✅ Correlation ID filter - automatic
[codex] - ⚙️ HTTP logging - opt-in via `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.enabled=true`; structured text bodies are redacted and unsafe body types are omitted
[codex] - ⚙️ OpenAPI - extend `BaseOpenApiConfig` with `@Configuration` + `@OpenApiDefinition`
[codex] 
[codex] **IMPORTANT**: Component scanning of `org.budgetanalyzer.service` is **NOT required** for autoconfiguration. The Spring Boot autoconfiguration mechanism handles everything automatically via `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Architectural Principles
[codex] 
[codex] ### Architectural Simplicity (KISS)
[codex] 
[codex] **Primary rule: Keep it simple.** Choose the simplest implementation that correctly handles realistic inputs, states, and failure modes. Simplicity must not come at the expense of security, data integrity, or required behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] - Put validation in the layer that owns the rule: request models and controllers validate request shape and syntax; services validate business invariants, ownership, persistence state, and cross-entity rules.
[codex] - Do not duplicate API validation in the service layer when every call reaches the service through the validated API contract. Add service-level validation when another caller can bypass that contract or when the service owns the rule.
[codex] - Do not add a guard, fallback, or custom exception path for a state made impossible by an enforced boundary or invariant.
[codex] - At external or asynchronous boundaries, handle plausible failures explicitly because they are outside the local code's control.
[codex] - Before adding a defensive branch, identify how the state can arise and what the caller or system can usefully do in response. If neither is concrete, omit the branch.
[codex] - Prefer a direct implementation and established project patterns over speculative abstractions or extension points.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Production-Quality Code
[codex] All code must be production-ready. No shortcuts, prototypes, or workarounds.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to read more**: [docs/common-patterns.md](docs/common-patterns.md) - SOLID principles, design patterns, Spring Boot patterns, database patterns, security/performance best practices.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Pure JPA Only
[codex] **CRITICAL**: Use pure JPA (Jakarta Persistence API) exclusively. NO Hibernate-specific features.
[codex] - Forbidden: `org.hibernate.*`
[codex] - Allowed: `jakarta.persistence.*`
[codex] 
[codex] ### Vendor Independence
[codex] **CRITICAL**: Avoid lock-in to external providers. Abstract all external dependencies behind interfaces. Internal identifiers must be provider-agnostic.
[codex] 
[codex] **Applies to**: Auth providers (e.g., Auth0, Okta), data providers (FRED), cloud services (AWS/GCP), payment processors, email services, SMS gateways — any external system.
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Service → Interface → Provider Implementation → External Client. The interface is yours; the implementation is swappable.
[codex] 
[codex] **Identifiers**: Use prefixed string IDs (`usr_`, `txn_`, `req_`) as foreign keys and in cross-service communication. These IDs flow across service boundaries and into JWTs — auto-increment longs couple identity to a single database; string UUIDs are portable by default. Prefixes make IDs self-describing in logs, JWTs, and database queries. Always use full UUIDs (32 hex chars) — never truncate (entropy reduction with no storage benefit since the type is `String` either way). Format: `{prefix}_{full-uuid-hex}` (e.g., `usr_507f1f77bcf86cd799439011abcdef12`). Translate external IDs (IdP sub, Stripe customer ID) at the boundary. Never let external ID formats leak into your domain model.
[codex] 
[codex] **Why**: The cost of abstraction is bounded (one interface, one translation layer). The cost of provider migration without abstraction is unbounded (every table, every service, every log).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Standardized Error Responses
[codex] All API errors follow `ApiErrorResponse` format with error types, field-level validation, and error codes.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Soft Delete Pattern
[codex] Entities extending `SoftDeletableEntity` are never actually deleted from the database - only marked as deleted.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Backwards Compatibility: CI/CD-Driven Lockstep Upgrades
[codex] **CRITICAL**: ALL changes to spring-platform, spring-cloud-platform, service-core, and service-web MUST be backwards compatible. We maintain a common platform across all microservices and upgrade all services in lockstep when we upgrade these artifacts.
[codex] 
[codex] All four artifacts are versioned and released together as a coordinated set.
[codex] 
[codex] **When to consult details**:
[codex] - Determining if a change is breaking → Read [What's Breaking vs. Safe](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#examples-whats-breaking-vs-safe)
[codex] - Deprecating APIs → Read [Deprecation Process](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#3-deprecate-before-removal)
[codex] - Database schema changes → Read [Database Migrations](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#database-migrations)
[codex] - Version bumps → Read [Semantic Versioning](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#semantic-versioning) and [Version Bump Checklist](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md#version-bump-checklist)
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference**:
[codex] - Add new, don't modify existing (extend rather than change)
[codex] - Platform changes can be breaking when they remove constraints or move major framework versions
[codex] - Prefer expand/contract database migrations; incompatible schema or data changes cannot rely on default Kubernetes rolling updates
[codex] - Test against all consuming services before release
[codex] - Major version bumps require coordinated migration across all services
[codex] 
[codex] **For comprehensive compatibility guidelines, read [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md) when making any changes to service-common artifacts.**
[codex] 
[codex] ### Code Quality Standards
[codex] - **Spotless**: Google Java Format
[codex] - **Checkstyle**: Custom rules (including Hibernate import ban)
[codex] - **Variable declarations**: Use `var` whenever possible
[codex] - **Variable naming**: Class-level fields MUST use full class name (e.g., `currencySeriesRepository` not `repository`)
[codex] - **Imports**: No wildcard imports, always explicit
[codex] - **Javadoc**: First sentence MUST end with period (`.`)
[codex] 
[codex] **When to read more**: [docs/code-quality-standards.md](docs/code-quality-standards.md) - Complete Spotless/Checkstyle configuration, detailed formatting rules, IDE setup, troubleshooting.
[codex] 
[codex] **Build commands**:
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Always run these two commands in sequence
[codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Advanced Patterns
[codex] 
[codex] **When to use advanced patterns**: External integrations, messaging, caching, distributed systems, scheduled tasks.
[codex] 
[codex] **Pattern**: Provider abstraction (external APIs), Transactional Outbox (guaranteed messaging), Redis caching (performance), ShedLock (distributed locking), Flyway (database migrations).
[codex] 
[codex] **When to consult details**:
[codex] - Integrating external APIs → Read [Provider Abstraction Pattern](docs/advanced-patterns.md#provider-abstraction-pattern)
[codex] - Event-driven messaging → Read [Transactional Outbox](docs/advanced-patterns.md#event-driven-messaging-with-transactional-outbox)
[codex] - Implementing caching → Read [Redis Distributed Caching](docs/advanced-patterns.md#redis-distributed-caching)
[codex] - Scheduled tasks in multi-pod deployment → Read [ShedLock](docs/advanced-patterns.md#distributed-locking-with-shedlock)
[codex] - Database schema changes → Read [Flyway Migrations](docs/advanced-patterns.md#database-migrations-with-flyway)
[codex] 
[codex] **Quick reference**:
[codex] - Provider Pattern: Service → Interface → Implementation → External Client (decouples from external dependencies)
[codex] - Transactional Outbox: Events persisted in DB with business data, 100% guaranteed delivery to RabbitMQ
[codex] - Redis Caching: `@Cacheable` on queries, `@CacheEvict` on updates (50-200x faster responses)
[codex] - ShedLock: `@SchedulerLock` on `@Scheduled` methods (runs once across all pods)
[codex] - Flyway: `V{version}__{description}.sql` migrations (never modify committed migrations)
[codex] 
[codex] **For advanced implementation details, read [docs/advanced-patterns.md](docs/advanced-patterns.md) when implementing these features.**
[codex] 
[codex] ## Best Practices
[codex] 
[codex] ### Adding to service-common
[codex] 
[codex] **When to add**:
[codex] - Used by 2+ services
[codex] - Cross-cutting concern (logging, error handling, security)
[codex] - Common DTOs or utilities
[codex] 
[codex] **When NOT to add**:
[codex] - Service-specific business logic
[codex] - One-off utilities
[codex] - Domain models (unless truly shared)
[codex] 
[codex] **Process**:
[codex] 1. Verify need (is this really shared?)
[codex] 2. Add to service-common with tests
[codex] 3. Version bump (semantic versioning)
[codex] 4. Publish locally with `./gradlew publishToMavenLocal`; use the versioning doc for remote or release publishing
[codex] 5. Update consuming services
[codex] 6. Document changes
[codex] 
[codex] ### Library Design Principles
[codex] 
[codex] **Backward compatibility** - ALL changes must be backwards compatible to support lockstep upgrades across all microservices. Breaking changes force version fragmentation and deployment coordination problems. We upgrade all services together when we upgrade service-common to catch integration issues immediately and maintain a common platform. Add new methods/classes rather than modifying existing ones. Major version bumps require coordinated migration across all services.
[codex] 
[codex] **Minimal dependencies** - Only include essential dependencies
[codex] 
[codex] **Clear package separation** - Core (domain-agnostic) vs. Service (Spring-specific)
[codex] 
[codex] **Comprehensive Javadoc** - All public APIs must be documented
[codex] 
[codex] ## NOTES FOR AI AGENTS
[codex] 
[codex] **CRITICAL - Prerequisites First**: Before implementing any plan or feature:
[codex] 1. Check for prerequisites in documentation (e.g., "Prerequisites: service-common Enhancement")
[codex] 2. If prerequisites are NOT satisfied, STOP immediately and inform the user
[codex] 3. Do NOT attempt to hack around missing prerequisites - this leads to broken implementations that must be deleted
[codex] 4. Complete prerequisites first, then return to the original task
[codex] 
[codex] ### Critical Rules
[codex] **All code must be production-quality** - No shortcuts, prototypes, or workarounds
[codex] **Use pure JPA only** - No Hibernate-specific imports (`org.hibernate.*`)
[codex] **Maintain backwards compatibility** - All service-common changes must work with existing consuming services (lockstep upgrade strategy)
[codex] **Always run build commands**:
[codex]    ```bash
[codex]    ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex]    ./gradlew clean build
[codex]    ```
[codex] **Fix Checkstyle warnings** - Treat warnings as errors requiring immediate resolution
[codex] 
[codex] ### Common Checkstyle Issues
[codex] - Javadoc missing periods at end of first sentence
[codex] - Wildcard imports (use explicit imports)
[codex] - Hibernate imports (forbidden)
[codex] - Missing Javadoc on public methods/classes
[codex] 
[codex] ### Testing Requirements
[codex] - Write tests for all new features
[codex] - Minimum 80% coverage
[codex] - Test edge cases explicitly
[codex] - Test correct behavior, not defective implementations (fix bugs, don't test around them)
[codex] - **NEVER modify, weaken, or delete existing tests you did not write** to make new or changed tests pass — if existing tests break, fix your code or stop and ask the user
[codex]

[codex] ## Honest Discourse
[codex] 
[codex] Do not over-validate ideas. The user wants honest pushback, not agreement.

[codex] - Distinguish "novel" from "obvious in retrospect"
[codex] - Push back on vague claims — ask for concrete constraints
[codex] - Don't say "great question" or "that's a really interesting point"

[codex] ## Phase 3: Onboard service-common and its resolved Java graph
[codex] 
[codex] ### Workspace
[codex] 
[codex] ../service-common
[codex] 
[codex] ### Goal
[codex] 
[codex] Discover platform/library updates and expose the actual BOM-managed dependency tree.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Scope
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate config, dependency-submission CI, and nearest repository documentation.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Non-goals
[codex] 
[codex] No BOM changes, overrides, library releases, build-logic redesign, or sibling edits.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Required context
[codex] 
[codex] Read this repository's `AGENTS.md`, version catalog, modules and build workflows,
[codex] plus orchestration's shared preset and service-common artifact-resolution guide.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Execution steps
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Add a thin Renovate config extending the shared preset. Verify extraction from
[codex]    catalogs, Gradle plugins, wrapper, Dockerfiles and Actions where present.
[codex] 2. Add official Gradle dependency submission on trusted default-branch pushes and
[codex]    a weekly/manual refresh. Use `cache-provider: basic`; submit with the job's
[codex]    GitHub token and the narrowly required `contents: write` permission.
[codex] 3. Resolve all relevant modules/configurations, including runtime and test trees.
[codex]    Record evidence that Framework, Security, Tomcat, Netty and Jackson appear
[codex]    where actually resolved, not just the imported Boot BOM coordinate.
[codex] 4. Document inherited-dependency limits, visible major migrations and bot PR checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Implementation notes
[codex] 
[codex] Use the existing Node 24-ready Actions baseline. Keep submission on trusted code;
[codex] do not publish packages or enable Build Scan services to obtain a dependency graph.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Validate Renovate config, inspect extraction/lookup, and run actionlint. Generate
[codex] the official dependency snapshot locally where supported without submitting it;
[codex] check module and transitive coverage. Hosted submission is verified after activation.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Completion criteria
[codex] 
[codex] The graph-generation workflow and bot config are reviewable, and local evidence
[codex] shows resolved transitive coverage without changing the selected versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Phase 4: Onboard currency-service
[codex] 
[codex] ### Workspace
[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 
[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] for the whole Budget Analyzer stack. Bump and release it when
[codex] `spring-platform`, `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, or `service-web`
[codex] changes. Do not change it only because a service image, frontend artifact,
[codex] route, production overlay, `/api-docs` metadata file, or OCI deployment
[codex] configuration changed.
[codex] 
[codex] Java services can release runtime artifacts while continuing to consume the
[codex] same checked-in `serviceCommon` coordinate from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`.
[codex] The production deployment identity is the digest-pinned image inventory and
[codex] deployment manifest, which may contain mixed service artifact versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local Contributor Flow
[codex] 
[codex] The supported side-by-side workspace flow must stay credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] If you follow [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and run local
[codex] development through orchestration and Tilt:
[codex] 
[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 
[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 
[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 
[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex]   Local
[codex] 
[codex] If you hit a local `service-common` resolution error while working in the full
[codex] workspace, republish it locally and rerun the service build:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../service-common
[codex] ./gradlew clean build publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When GitHub Packages Credentials Are Required
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Packages credentials are a CI/release concern, not a getting-started
[codex] prerequisite.
[codex] 
[codex] You need GitHub Packages credentials when all of these are true:
[codex] 
[codex] - the build is not using the local orchestration/Tilt path
[codex] - `service-common` is not already available through Maven Local
[codex] - the build must resolve the published `org.budgetanalyzer` artifacts remotely
[codex]   from `https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common`
[codex] 
[codex] Typical examples:
[codex] 
[codex] - default GitHub Actions `build.yml` runs in `transaction-service`,
[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 
[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 
[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex]   Packages
[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 
[codex] ## GitHub Actions Environment
[codex] 
[codex] When a workflow intentionally resolves `service-common` remotely from GitHub
[codex] Packages, Gradle needs both a token and a package username.
[codex] 
[codex] For cross-repo private Maven/Gradle consumption, do not assume the workflow
[codex] repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can read `service-common`. GitHub still treats these
[codex] packages as repository-scoped, so the consuming workflow needs an explicit
[codex] GitHub Packages credential.
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the current repo wiring expects:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `GITHUB_ACTOR` here means the username that owns the GitHub Packages credential,
[codex] not necessarily `${{ github.actor }}`. `GITHUB_TOKEN` here means the secret used
[codex] for remote package reads, not necessarily the workflow repo's default token.
[codex] This requirement applies to the remote GitHub Packages path used by default
[codex] `build.yml` and release workflows. It does not apply to the normal `tilt up`
[codex] contributor flow.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Containerized Release Builds
[codex] 
[codex] The Java service Dockerfiles now support remote `service-common` resolution for
[codex] release and isolated CI builds without copying host `.m2` state into the build
[codex] context and without checking out sibling source trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Pass the workflow credentials into BuildKit as secrets:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] docker build \
[codex]   --secret id=github_actor,env=GITHUB_ACTOR \
[codex]   --secret id=github_token,env=GITHUB_TOKEN \
[codex]   -f Dockerfile .
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the release job should still expose those same credential
[codex] values under the env names the current Gradle builds read:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The Dockerfiles read those values only inside the Gradle builder-stage `RUN`
[codex] steps, so the credentials are not written into the image, filesystem layers, or
[codex] checked-in repo files.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Why Public Package Visibility Does Not Remove This
[codex] 
[codex] Making the `service-common` Maven packages public in the GitHub UI does not
[codex] remove the Maven/Gradle authentication requirement.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep the release workflow package-visibility and workflow-access cleanup, but do not
[codex] treat it as the fix for local contributor UX. The fix for local contributor UX
[codex] is keeping the side-by-side workspace and Tilt flow local-first and
[codex] credential-free.
