[codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] 
[codex] **Status:** Configuration prepared; administrator activation is still required.
[codex] 
[codex] This document owns the operating policy for dependency automation across the
[codex] Budget Analyzer repositories. The preserved
[codex] [September 6 dependency review](research/dependency-update-review-2026-09-06.md)
[codex] is the historical acceptance benchmark; its observed versions are not bot
[codex] targets. The executable rollout and acceptance sequence remains in the
[codex] [dependency automation plan](plans/dependency-automation-plan.md).
[codex] 
[codex] ## Ownership and policy
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate is the only service allowed to open dependency update pull requests.
[codex] Dependabot remains alert-only: keep the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts
[codex] enabled, disable Dependabot version updates and overlapping Dependabot security
[codex] update pull requests, and let Renovate consume alerts when its App permission
[codex] allows that. Never enable automerge, including for security fixes.
[codex] 
[codex] The shared policy is
[codex] [`renovate-presets/default.json`](../renovate-presets/default.json). Repository
[codex] configuration extends it and adds only repository-specific manager paths and
[codex] extraction rules. Routine pull requests are created weekly, with no more than
[codex] three open routine pull requests and two new routine pull requests per hour in
[codex] one repository. Vulnerability fixes use Renovate's dedicated alert path and an
[codex] unrestricted schedule. Major, chart, stateful, platform, and checksum-coupled
[codex] updates require Dependency Dashboard approval.
[codex] 
[codex] Patch, minor, and major proposals remain distinct. Digest refreshes must retain
[codex] an immutable digest and any image flavor suffix. Reviewers must independently
[codex] verify Linux ARM64 support. An available tag or a changed digest is not evidence
[codex] of architecture support, embedded package versions, vulnerability remediation,
[codex] or upstream support status.
[codex] 
[codex] The following stay outside Renovate ownership:
[codex] 
[codex] - historical/generated outputs and the retained stale test suites
[codex] - local `:latest` and generated `:tilt-*` images listed by the image-pinning
[codex]   inventories
[codex] - first-party production images and their synchronized manifest, inventory,
[codex]   overlay, runtime metadata, and API-docs metadata
[codex] - Pod Security compatibility labels
[codex] - arbitrary per-platform checksum calculation
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate may propose checksum-coupled tool versions, but dashboard approval and
[codex] the existing checksum verification remain mandatory. A reviewer must update the
[codex] complete checksum table; no validation may be weakened to merge the proposal.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Cost and service boundary
[codex] 
[codex] Use the open-source Renovate engine through the free Mend Renovate Community
[codex] GitHub App. Do not provision a self-hosted bot or opt into paid Merge Confidence,
[codex] enterprise scheduling, enhanced cache, a trial, or another subscription. The
[codex] selected security evidence uses GitHub's dependency graph and Dependabot alerts,
[codex] Gradle's open-source basic dependency-submission path, Trivy, `npm audit`, and
[codex] `govulncheck`.
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Actions usage is separate from Mend hosting. Public repositories can use
[codex] standard hosted Linux runners. Before enabling automation for a private
[codex] repository, confirm its included Actions allowance and that paid overages are
[codex] disabled. Stop for user direction if required coverage needs spending or a
[codex] capability unavailable on the free offering.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Activation procedure
[codex] 
[codex] Configuration preparation does not authorize GitHub setting changes. A
[codex] repository administrator performs activation only after all rollout phases have
[codex] prepared and validated their files:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Publish the orchestration repository and its shared preset before publishing
[codex]    consumer configurations.
[codex] 2. At the start of Phase 12, manually dispatch
[codex]    `dependency-automation-config.yml` with `run_hosted_dry_run` enabled. Its
[codex]    GitHub-provided job token has read permissions only, Renovate runs with
[codex]    `--dry-run=full`, and the user retains control of the trigger. Preserve the
[codex]    workflow URL and confirm that the published preset resolves before proceeding;
[codex]    never copy the job token into an agent environment.
[codex] 3. Confirm repository visibility, Actions billing/overage settings, and that the
[codex]    free Mend service supports every scoped repository and required authenticated
[codex]    Maven lookup within its job limits.
[codex] 4. Install the Renovate Community App only on the repositories listed in the
[codex]    rollout plan. Grant read access to Dependabot alerts when available.
[codex] 5. Enable the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts. Disable Dependabot version
[codex]    updates and automatic Dependabot security-update pull requests.
[codex] 6. Configure private Maven access only through Mend-supported encrypted secrets
[codex]    and host rules. Never commit a token and never use `pull_request_target` to
[codex]    expose trusted credentials to dependency branches.
[codex] 7. Run onboarding for orchestration first. Inspect the resolved configuration,
[codex]    extraction logs, dashboard, job duration, and any rate-limit or timeout
[codex]    message before enabling consumers.
[codex] 8. Record every baseline item in the coverage report as reproduced, superseded,
[codex]    false positive with evidence, or missing. Update discovery, advisory evidence,
[codex]    and lifecycle assessment are separate results.
[codex] 
[codex] The Community service's documented single concurrent organization job,
[codex] approximately four-hour scheduling, and 30-minute timeout are acceptable only if
[codex] the pilot completes in practice. A timeout or unsupported authenticated lookup
[codex] blocks broader activation; it is not a reason to omit dependencies.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local validation and dry run
[codex] 
[codex] The focused workflow validates both JSON files with Renovate's official
[codex] `renovate-config-validator` under Node 24. Before publishing the preset, run the
[codex] same strict schema validation locally. For an unpublished-preset dry run, load
[codex] `renovate-presets/default.json` as the local self-hosted configuration, suppress
[codex] repository config discovery, and process the checkout with the local platform.
[codex] Use debug logging and do not provide a GitHub token or enable a write-capable
[codex] platform mode. Local mode proves extraction and supported lookups but does not
[codex] perform a true full dry run. Phase 12 owns the post-publication GitHub-platform
[codex] full dry run through the manually dispatched, read-only workflow; its ephemeral
[codex] job token is never provided to an agent.
[codex] 
[codex] Extraction and lookup evidence belongs in
[codex] [`docs/research/dependency-automation-coverage.md`](research/dependency-automation-coverage.md),
[codex] not in a second desired-version inventory.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Exact-image security evidence
[codex] 
[codex] `exact-image-security-evidence.yml` runs weekly and by manual dispatch. It has
[codex] no pull-request trigger and findings do not create a merge gate. The job fails
[codex] when rendering, registry resolution, database download, package inventory, or a
[codex] vulnerability scan is incomplete; vulnerability findings themselves leave the
[codex] scan successful. The final upload runs even after failure and retains evidence
[codex] for seven days without relying on SARIF hosting or a paid service.
[codex] 
[codex] The workflow calls `scripts/security/render-image-scan-inputs.sh`, which uses
[codex] the production Kustomize overlays, controller chart versions from
[codex] `deploy/scripts/lib/version-contract.sh`, their checked-in values, and the
[codex] Prometheus/Kiali post-renderers. It includes Helm-created controllers and hooks,
[codex] operator-supplied cert-manager solver and Prometheus config-reloader images,
[codex] standalone Jaeger and infrastructure images, and the Tilt Temurin, Kind, and
[codex] frontend production-smoke images. First-party service images remain under the
[codex] release workflow and are excluded from this scanner job.
[codex] 
[codex] Production/controller images are resolved and scanned as `linux/arm64`.
[codex] Local-only sources use the actual scanner-runner platform (`linux/amd64` on the
[codex] hosted job). For each deduplicated target, the artifact contains:
[codex] 
[codex] - the rendered source ref and every file that contributed it
[codex] - the registry manifest and the selected platform child digest
[codex] - a Trivy all-package JSON inventory with OS and application package classes
[codex] - a separate JSON vulnerability report plus captured stderr
[codex] - Trivy CLI/database and registry-resolver metadata, database-download logs,
[codex]   and a machine-readable completion status
[codex] 
[codex] A rendered tag without a digest is labelled `mutable` even though the job
[codex] immediately resolves and scans one exact platform digest. This preserves the
[codex] difference between repeatable evidence for that run and immutable checked-in
[codex] desired state. In particular, External Secrets chart 2.2.0 currently renders
[codex] `ghcr.io/external-secrets/external-secrets:v2.2.0`; its checked-in `image.digest`
[codex] value is not emitted by that chart version. The tag resolved to the intended
[codex] digest during Phase 2 validation, but later tag movement remains possible.
[codex] The cert-manager controller/startup refs and Istio CNI/pilot refs are likewise
[codex] tag-only chart output; only their resolved artifact for a particular run is
[codex] exact. The cert-manager ACME solver input does retain its checked-in digest.
[codex] 
[codex] The existing `deploy/scripts/render/observability.sh` and
[codex] `guardrails/verify-production-image-overlay.sh` are not CI render entry points:
[codex] they intentionally use a live server-side Helm dry run for Kiali RBAC. The
[codex] scanner invokes ordinary `helm template` with the same Kiali values and
[codex] post-renderer, so it needs no Kubernetes API or production credentials. One
[codex] known gap remains explicit: the Istio gateway chart renders `image: auto`, and
[codex] only a live Istio injector chooses the proxy image. The workflow records that
[codex] target as a known unscannable gap rather than adding a live-cluster dependency
[codex] or claiming coverage. Unexpected render, resolution, database, or scan failures
[codex] still fail the job.
[codex] 
[codex] Trivy package evidence is not a lifecycle authority and does not guarantee that
[codex] an image exposes its primary application version. The coverage report records
[codex] known Redis, Temurin, and controller-binary inventory limitations. Never infer a
[codex] Redis patch or Java CPU level from the broad source tag or from a successful OS
[codex] package scan.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Failure triage
[codex] 
[codex] - **Configuration error:** reproduce with the pinned validator version. Treat
[codex]   warnings and migrations as failures; correct the preset or repository config.
[codex] - **No extraction:** check the owning file's native manager pattern or adjacent
[codex]   annotation and compare debug logs. Do not seed the historical expected version
[codex]   into configuration.
[codex] - **No lookup or wrong package:** verify the real datasource identity, chart
[codex]   name, registry URL, tag prefix, build suffix, and flavor suffix. Record an
[codex]   unresolved standard-tool gap instead of adding a custom crawler.
[codex] - **Authentication or resolution failure:** preserve the failure. Fix encrypted
[codex]   Maven/registry access in the supported trust boundary; do not use Maven Local,
[codex]   drop internal dependencies, or swallow the error.
[codex] - **Finding without an update:** retain the Dependabot/scanner finding and route
[codex]   inherited or transitive remediation to the owning service. An update pull
[codex]   request is not proof that an advisory was detected.
[codex] - **CI failure on a bot branch:** determine whether the proposal needs its
[codex]   documented checksum, chart rendering, ARM64, state migration, or cross-repo
[codex]   companion review. Do not relax a guardrail or create orchestration drift.
[codex] - **Scanner database/download failure:** report the job as failed separately
[codex]   from vulnerability findings. Never turn an unavailable scan into a clean result.
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate discovery and vulnerability scanners do not comprehensively establish
[codex] end-of-life status. Owners must still perform a quarterly support-policy review
[codex] against upstream lifecycle sources.

[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 
[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] for the whole Budget Analyzer stack. Bump and release it when
[codex] `spring-platform`, `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, or `service-web`
[codex] changes. Do not change it only because a service image, frontend artifact,
[codex] route, production overlay, `/api-docs` metadata file, or OCI deployment
[codex] configuration changed.
[codex] 
[codex] Java services can release runtime artifacts while continuing to consume the
[codex] same checked-in `serviceCommon` coordinate from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`.
[codex] The production deployment identity is the digest-pinned image inventory and
[codex] deployment manifest, which may contain mixed service artifact versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local Contributor Flow
[codex] 
[codex] The supported side-by-side workspace flow must stay credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] If you follow [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and run local
[codex] development through orchestration and Tilt:
[codex] 
[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 
[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 
[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 
[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex]   Local
[codex] 
[codex] If you hit a local `service-common` resolution error while working in the full
[codex] workspace, republish it locally and rerun the service build:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../service-common
[codex] ./gradlew clean build publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When GitHub Packages Credentials Are Required
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Packages credentials are a CI/release concern, not a getting-started
[codex] prerequisite.
[codex] 
[codex] You need GitHub Packages credentials when all of these are true:
[codex] 
[codex] - the build is not using the local orchestration/Tilt path
[codex] - `service-common` is not already available through Maven Local
[codex] - the build must resolve the published `org.budgetanalyzer` artifacts remotely
[codex]   from `https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common`
[codex] 
[codex] Typical examples:
[codex] 
[codex] - default GitHub Actions `build.yml` runs in `transaction-service`,
[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 
[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 
[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex]   Packages
[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 
[codex] ## GitHub Actions Environment
[codex] 
[codex] When a workflow intentionally resolves `service-common` remotely from GitHub
[codex] Packages, Gradle needs both a token and a package username.
[codex] 
[codex] For cross-repo private Maven/Gradle consumption, do not assume the workflow
[codex] repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can read `service-common`. GitHub still treats these
[codex] packages as repository-scoped, so the consuming workflow needs an explicit
[codex] GitHub Packages credential.
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the current repo wiring expects:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `GITHUB_ACTOR` here means the username that owns the GitHub Packages credential,
[codex] not necessarily `${{ github.actor }}`. `GITHUB_TOKEN` here means the secret used
[codex] for remote package reads, not necessarily the workflow repo's default token.
[codex] This requirement applies to the remote GitHub Packages path used by default
[codex] `build.yml` and release workflows. It does not apply to the normal `tilt up`
[codex] contributor flow.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Containerized Release Builds
[codex] 
[codex] The Java service Dockerfiles now support remote `service-common` resolution for
[codex] release and isolated CI builds without copying host `.m2` state into the build
[codex] context and without checking out sibling source trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Pass the workflow credentials into BuildKit as secrets:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] docker build \
[codex]   --secret id=github_actor,env=GITHUB_ACTOR \
[codex]   --secret id=github_token,env=GITHUB_TOKEN \
[codex]   -f Dockerfile .
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the release job should still expose those same credential
[codex] values under the env names the current Gradle builds read:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The Dockerfiles read those values only inside the Gradle builder-stage `RUN`
[codex] steps, so the credentials are not written into the image, filesystem layers, or
[codex] checked-in repo files.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Why Public Package Visibility Does Not Remove This
[codex] 
[codex] Making the `service-common` Maven packages public in the GitHub UI does not
[codex] remove the Maven/Gradle authentication requirement.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep the release workflow package-visibility and workflow-access cleanup, but do not
[codex] treat it as the fix for local contributor UX. The fix for local contributor UX
[codex] is keeping the side-by-side workspace and Tilt flow local-first and
[codex] credential-free.

[codex]             )
[codex]

[codex] name: Publish Release
[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     tags:
[codex]       - 'v*'
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: read
[codex] 
[codex] env:
[codex]   FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] 
[codex] jobs:
[codex]   publish:
[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex]     permissions:
[codex]       contents: read
[codex]       packages: write
[codex]     env:
[codex]       GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ github.actor }}
[codex]       GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
[codex] 
[codex]     steps:
[codex]       - name: Checkout code
[codex]         uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up JDK 25
[codex]         uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex]         with:
[codex]           java-version: '25'
[codex]           distribution: 'temurin'
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Setup Gradle
[codex]         uses: gradle/actions/setup-gradle@v6
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Verify tag matches build version
[codex]         id: version
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           tag_version="${GITHUB_REF_NAME#v}"
[codex]           build_version="$(sed -nE 's/^[[:space:]]*version = \"([^\"]+)\"$/\1/p' build.gradle.kts | head -n1)"
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${build_version}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Could not read the checked-in version literal from build.gradle.kts." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${tag_version}" != "${build_version}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Release tag ${GITHUB_REF_NAME} does not match build.gradle.kts version ${build_version}." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           echo "build_version=${build_version}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Publish to GitHub Packages Maven
[codex]         run: ./gradlew publish
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Report published package links
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         env:
[codex]           BUILD_VERSION: ${{ steps.version.outputs.build_version }}
[codex]           GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           packages=(
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.spring-platform
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.spring-cloud-platform
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.service-core
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.service-web
[codex]           )
[codex] 
[codex]           echo "Published service-common ${BUILD_VERSION} packages:"
[codex]           {
[codex]             echo "## Published service-common ${BUILD_VERSION}"
[codex]             echo
[codex]             echo "| Artifact | GitHub Packages |"
[codex]             echo "| --- | --- |"
[codex]           } >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] 
[codex]           for package_name in "${packages[@]}"; do
[codex]             package_url="$(
[codex]               gh api --paginate "/orgs/budgetanalyzer/packages/maven/${package_name}/versions" \
[codex]                 --jq ".[] | select(.name == \"${BUILD_VERSION}\") | .html_url" \
[codex]                 | head -n1
[codex]             )"
[codex] 
[codex]             if [[ -z "${package_url}" ]]; then
[codex]               echo "Could not resolve GitHub Packages URL for ${package_name} ${BUILD_VERSION}." >&2
[codex]               exit 1
[codex]             fi
[codex] 
[codex]             echo "- ${package_name}: ${package_url}"
[codex]             echo "| ${package_name} | [${package_url}](${package_url}) |" >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex]           done
[codex] FILE: .github/workflows/publish-snapshot.yml
[codex] name: Publish Snapshot
[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     branches: [main]
[codex]   workflow_dispatch:
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: read
[codex] 
[codex] env:
[codex]   FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] 
[codex] jobs:
[codex]   publish:
[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex]     permissions:
[codex]       contents: read
[codex]       packages: write
[codex]     env:
[codex]       GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ github.actor }}
[codex]       GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
[codex] 
[codex]     steps:
[codex]       - name: Checkout code
[codex]         uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Read checked-in version
[codex]         id: version
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           build_version="$(sed -nE 's/^[[:space:]]*version = \"([^\"]+)\"$/\1/p' build.gradle.kts | head -n1)"
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${build_version}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Could not read the checked-in version literal from build.gradle.kts." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           echo "build_version=${build_version}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${build_version}" == *"-SNAPSHOT" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "should_publish=true" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex]           else
[codex]             echo "should_publish=false" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Skip non-snapshot versions
[codex]         if: steps.version.outputs.should_publish != 'true'
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           echo "Checked-in version ${BUILD_VERSION} is not a snapshot; skipping snapshot publish."
[codex]         env:
[codex]           BUILD_VERSION: ${{ steps.version.outputs.build_version }}
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up JDK 25
[codex]         if: steps.version.outputs.should_publish == 'true'
[codex]         uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex]         with:
[codex]           java-version: '25'
[codex]           distribution: 'temurin'
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Setup Gradle
[codex]         if: steps.version.outputs.should_publish == 'true'
[codex]         uses: gradle/actions/setup-gradle@v6
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Publish snapshot to GitHub Packages Maven
[codex]         if: steps.version.outputs.should_publish == 'true'
[codex]         run: ./gradlew publish
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Report published package links
[codex]         if: steps.version.outputs.should_publish == 'true'
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         env:
[codex]           BUILD_VERSION: ${{ steps.version.outputs.build_version }}
[codex]           GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           packages=(
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.spring-platform
[codex]             org.budgetanalyzer.spring-cloud-platform

[codex] FILE: README.md
[codex] # Service Common
[codex] 
[codex] > "Archetype: platform. Role: Shared library; provides patterns all Java services consume."
[codex] >
[codex] > — [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#tree-position)
[codex] 
[codex] [![Build](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common/actions/workflows/build.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common/actions/workflows/build.yml)
[codex] 
[codex] Shared libraries for Budget Analyzer microservices - a personal finance management application.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Overview
[codex] 
[codex] Service Common is a **multi-module Gradle project** that provides common functionality shared across all Budget Analyzer backend microservices. It publishes four artifacts:
[codex] 
[codex] ### spring-platform
[codex] **Purpose**: Base dependency platform for Budget Analyzer Spring services.
[codex] 
[codex] **Provides**:
[codex] - Spring Boot dependency BOM import
[codex] - Spring Modulith dependency BOM import
[codex] - Shared version constraints for SpringDoc, Testcontainers, WireMock, Awaitility, and ShedLock
[codex] 
[codex] **Behavior**: Version management only. It does not add runtime dependencies, application code, or autoconfiguration.
[codex] 
[codex] ### spring-cloud-platform
[codex] **Purpose**: Opt-in Spring Cloud dependency platform for services that use Spring Cloud APIs.
[codex] 
[codex] **Provides**:
[codex] - spring-platform import
[codex] - Spring Cloud dependency BOM import
[codex] 
[codex] **Behavior**: Version management only. Non-Cloud services should use spring-platform instead.
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-core
[codex] **Purpose**: Minimal-dependency core utilities for microservices
[codex] 
[codex] **Features**:
[codex] - Base JPA entity classes (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity)
[codex] - JPA entity listeners and repository utilities
[codex] - CSV parsing capabilities (OpenCSV integration)
[codex] - Safe logging utilities with sensitive data masking
[codex] - Health checks and metrics (Spring Boot Actuator, Prometheus)
[codex] 
[codex] **Dependencies**: Spring Data JPA, Spring Boot Actuator, Jackson, SLF4J, OpenCSV
[codex] 
[codex] ### service-web
[codex] **Purpose**: Spring Boot web service components with auto-configuration for servlet and reactive stacks
[codex] 
[codex] **Features**:
[codex] - Standardized exception handling (@RestControllerAdvice)
[codex] - API error response models (ApiErrorResponse)
[codex] - HTTP request/response logging filters
[codex] - Correlation ID support
[codex] - OpenAPI/Swagger base configuration
[codex] 
[codex] **Dependencies**: service-core (transitive), Spring Boot Starter Web/WebFlux, SpringDoc OpenAPI
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: Actuator (health checks, metrics) comes from service-core and is available to all services (web and non-web).
[codex] 
[codex] **Note**: service-web includes service-core transitively, so most microservices only need to depend on service-web.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Purpose
[codex] 
[codex] Service Common promotes code reuse and consistency across microservices by:
[codex] - Reducing code duplication
[codex] - Ensuring consistent data models
[codex] - Providing shared utilities
[codex] - Standardizing error handling and validation
[codex] 
[codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] 
[codex] - **Java 25**
[codex] - **Spring Boot 3.x** (Starter Web, Data JPA)
[codex] - **SpringDoc OpenAPI** for API documentation
[codex] - **OpenCSV** for CSV file processing
[codex] - **JUnit 5** for testing
[codex] 
[codex] ## Usage
[codex] 
[codex] For local development, publish these artifacts to Maven Local and consume them from sibling
[codex] Budget Analyzer services. CI workflows publish snapshot and release artifacts to GitHub Packages.
[codex] Examples below use `&lt;service-common-version>` as a placeholder. Use the checked-in version literal
[codex] from `build.gradle.kts` for the build you are consuming.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Which Module Should I Use?
[codex] 
[codex] **Most microservices should use `service-web`** (which transitively includes `service-core`):
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Spring Cloud services should use `spring-cloud-platform`** instead of importing both platforms:
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-cloud-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Use `service-core` alone** only if you need minimal dependencies without Spring Web features:
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-core:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### Adding as a Dependency
[codex] 
[codex] In your service's `build.gradle.kts`:
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     // Import dependency versions explicitly.
[codex]     implementation(platform("org.budgetanalyzer:spring-platform:&lt;service-common-version>"))
[codex] 
[codex]     // Recommended: Use service-web (includes service-core transitively)
[codex]     implementation("org.budgetanalyzer:service-web:&lt;service-common-version>")
[codex] }
[codex] 
[codex] repositories {
[codex]     mavenLocal()
[codex]     mavenCentral()
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The platform artifacts only provide dependency version management. They do not provide beans,
[codex] filters, exception handlers, or autoconfiguration metadata. Runtime behavior comes from
[codex] `service-core` and `service-web`.
[codex] 
[codex] ### What You Get Automatically
[codex] 
[codex] The runtime modules use **Spring Boot autoconfiguration** - all components are automatically registered when the libraries are on your classpath. **No manual configuration or component scanning required!**
[codex] 
[codex] #### service-core (Fully Automatic)
[codex] When service-core is on your classpath, you automatically get:
[codex] - **JPA Entity Scanning** - Base entities (AuditableEntity, SoftDeletableEntity) automatically discovered
[codex] - **JPA Auditing** - Automatic timestamps (createdAt, updatedAt) on entities
[codex] - **CSV Parser Bean** - OpenCsvParser available for injection
[codex] - **Prometheus Metrics** - `/actuator/prometheus` endpoint exposed automatically for scraping
[codex] - **`application` Common Tag** - Every Micrometer meter is tagged with `application=${spring.application.name}` so metrics carry service identity independent of the scrape pipeline (override with `management.metrics.tags.application`)
[codex] 
[codex] #### service-web (Mostly Automatic)
[codex] When service-web is on your classpath, you automatically get:
[codex] - **Global Exception Handler** - All exceptions converted to standardized ApiErrorResponse format
[codex]   - Maps: 400 (Bad Request), 404 (Not Found), 422 (Business Logic), 500 (Server Error)
[codex] - **Claims Header Security** - Automatic stateless authentication from trusted ingress external-auth headers
[codex]   - Reads `X-User-Id`, `X-Permissions`, `X-Roles` headers for both servlet and reactive services
[codex]   - Public endpoints: health, prometheus, and OpenAPI routes; all other endpoints, including `/internal/**`, require service-owned rules or authenticated claims headers
[codex] - **Correlation ID Filter** - Automatically adds correlation IDs to all requests and regenerates malformed inbound values before they reach logs or response headers
[codex] - **HTTP Logging Filter** - Optional (enable with `budgetanalyzer.service.http-logging.enabled=true`)
[codex]   - Text bodies only: common secret fields in JSON and form payloads are redacted
[codex]   - Multipart, binary, and compressed bodies are omitted with placeholders instead of raw content
[codex]   - Required list settings and `log-level` must not be explicit null values; startup fails during
[codex]     configuration validation if they are bound as null. Empty lists are valid.
[codex] - **OpenAPI Base Config** - Standard error response schemas (extend BaseOpenApiConfig in your service)
[codex] 
[codex] **Example configuration** (application.yml):
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] budgetanalyzer:
[codex]   service:
[codex]     http-logging:
[codex]       enabled: true
[codex]       include-query-params: false # Optional: defaults to false
[codex]       include-request-body: true   # Optional: defaults to false; JSON/form secrets are redacted
[codex]       include-response-body: true  # Optional: defaults to false; binary/multipart/compressed bodies are omitted
[codex]       max-body-size: 10000         # Optional: values &lt;= 0 disable body capture
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **No component scanning needed** - Spring Boot autoconfiguration handles everything via `META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports`.
[codex] 
[codex] The shared claims-header security configuration provides the default Spring Security filter chain.
[codex] If a consuming service defines its own `SecurityFilterChain` or `SecurityWebFilterChain`, the
[codex] shared default chain backs off so the service's custom rules win deterministically.
[codex] 
[codex] #### Discovering What's Autoconfigured
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # View autoconfiguration registration files
[codex] cat service-core/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] cat service-web/src/main/resources/META-INF/spring/org.springframework.boot.autoconfigure.AutoConfiguration.imports
[codex] 
[codex] # View autoconfiguration classes
[codex] cat service-core/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/core/config/ServiceCoreAutoConfiguration.java
[codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/config/ServiceWebAutoConfiguration.java
[codex] 
[codex] # View global exception handler
[codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/servlet/api/ServletApiExceptionHandler.java
[codex] 
[codex] # View security configuration
[codex] cat service-web/src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/service/security/ClaimsHeaderSecurityConfig.java
[codex] 
[codex] # Find all @Configuration classes
[codex] grep -r "@Configuration\|@AutoConfiguration" service-*/src/main/java/
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] For complete details, see the [Autoconfiguration section in AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#autoconfiguration).
[codex] 
[codex] ### Building and Publishing
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Build all modules
[codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] 
[codex] # Publish all service-common artifacts to Maven Local for local development
[codex] ./gradlew publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `publishToMavenLocal` uses the checked-in version literal from
[codex] `build.gradle.kts` and is the supported local development path. It does not
[codex] require GitHub credentials and does not publish anything remotely. The build
[codex] workflow also runs `publishToMavenLocal` as a validation step only.
[codex] 
[codex] Remote publishing is handled by this repository's workflows:
[codex] `publish-snapshot.yml` publishes `-SNAPSHOT` versions from `main` to GitHub
[codex] Packages, and `publish-release.yml` publishes tag-driven releases to GitHub
[codex] Packages. Successful remote publishes write the published version and GitHub
[codex] Packages links to the workflow log and job summary. See
[codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md) for
[codex] the release and publishing contract.
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common`'s own version and backwards-compatibility contract lives in
[codex] [docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md](docs/versioning-and-compatibility.md).
[codex] 
[codex] ## Development
[codex] 
[codex] ### Prerequisites

[codex] ### Building
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Clean and build
[codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] 
[codex] # Run tests
[codex] ./gradlew test
[codex] 
[codex] # Run tests and generate JaCoCo coverage reports
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] 
[codex] # Check code style
[codex] ./gradlew spotlessCheck
[codex] 
[codex] # Apply code formatting
[codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] JaCoCo report generation is configured for the Java library modules only:
[codex] `service-core/build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] `service-web/build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html`. XML reports are written
[codex] next to the HTML reports for CI/dashboard ingestion. `check` enforces the
[codex] configured coverage gates.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Code Quality
[codex] 
[codex] This project enforces code quality through:
[codex] - **Google Java Format** for consistent code style
[codex] - **Checkstyle** for code standards
[codex] - **Spotless** for automated formatting
[codex] 
[codex] ## Conventions
[codex] 
[codex] ### Prefixed String IDs
[codex] 
[codex] All cross-service identifiers use the format `{prefix}_{full-uuid-hex}` (e.g., `usr_507f1f77bcf86cd799439011abcdef12`, `req_a1b2c3d4e5f6a7b8c9d0e1f2a3b4c5d6`).
[codex] 
[codex] - **Why strings over longs**: These IDs flow across service boundaries and into JWTs. Auto-increment longs couple identity to a single database; string UUIDs are portable by default.
[codex] - **Why prefixes**: `usr_`, `txn_`, `req_` make IDs self-describing in logs, JWTs, and database queries.
[codex] - **Why full UUIDs**: Always use 32 hex chars (128 bits). Never truncate — entropy reduction with no storage benefit since the type is `String` either way.
[codex] 
[codex] See the [Vendor Independence section in AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#vendor-independence) for the full rationale.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Project Structure
[codex] 
[codex] ```
[codex] service-common/
[codex] ├── spring-platform/                 # Base Spring dependency platform
[codex] │   └── build.gradle.kts
[codex] ├── spring-cloud-platform/           # Spring Cloud dependency platform overlay
[codex] │   └── build.gradle.kts
[codex] ├── service-core/                    # Core utilities module
[codex] │   ├── src/main/java/
[codex] │   │   └── org/budgetanalyzer/core/
[codex] │   │       ├── domain/              # Base JPA entities
[codex] │   │       ├── repository/          # Repository utilities
[codex] │   │       ├── csv/                 # CSV parsing
[codex] │   │       └── logging/             # Safe logging
[codex] │   ├── src/test/java/
[codex] │   └── build.gradle.kts
[codex] ├── service-web/                     # Spring Boot web module
[codex] │   ├── src/main/java/
[codex] │   │   └── org/budgetanalyzer/service/
[codex] │   │       ├── exception/           # Exception hierarchy
[codex] │   │       ├── api/                 # Error response models
[codex] │   │       ├── http/                # HTTP filters/logging
[codex] │   │       └── config/              # OpenAPI config
[codex] │   ├── src/test/java/
[codex] │   └── build.gradle.kts
[codex] ├── build.gradle.kts                 # Root build configuration
[codex] ├── settings.gradle.kts              # Multi-module setup
[codex] └── config/                          # Checkstyle configuration
[codex] ```

[codex] FILE: service-core/build.gradle.kts
[codex] description = "Core utilities for microservices - minimal dependencies"
[codex] 
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]     api(platform(project(":spring-platform")))
[codex] 
[codex]     // TRANSITIVE (api) - every service gets these
[codex]     api(libs.spring.boot.starter.actuator)
[codex]     api(libs.micrometer.registry.prometheus)
[codex] 
[codex]     // NOT TRANSITIVE (implementation) - services opt-in
[codex]     implementation(libs.jackson.databind)
[codex]     implementation(libs.jackson.datatype.jsr310)
[codex]     implementation(libs.slf4j.api)
[codex]

[codex]     compileOnly(libs.spring.boot.starter.data.jpa)
[codex]     compileOnly(libs.spring.security.core)
[codex]

[codex]

[codex] # The `dependency-submission` action
[codex] 
[codex] The `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action provides the simplest (and recommended) way to generate a 
[codex] dependency graph for your project. This action will attempt to detect all dependencies used by your build
[codex] without building and testing the project itself.
[codex] 
[codex] The dependency graph snapshot is generated via integration with the [GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin), and submitted to your repository via the 
[codex] [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] The generated snapshot files can be submitted in the same job, or saved for submission in a subsequent job.
[codex] 
[codex] The generated dependency graph includes all of the dependencies in your build, and is used by GitHub to generate 
[codex] [Dependabot Alerts](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/dependabot-alerts/about-dependabot-alerts) 
[codex] for vulnerable dependencies, as well as to populate the 
[codex] [Dependency Graph insights view](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/supply-chain-security/understanding-your-software-supply-chain/exploring-the-dependencies-of-a-repository#viewing-the-dependency-graph).
[codex] 
[codex] If you're confused by the behaviour you're seeing or have specific questions, please check out [the FAQ](dependency-submission-faq.md) before raising an issue.
[codex] 
[codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] > ### ⚡️ Choice of caching providers in v6
[codex] > To provide the fastest possible build experience this action includes **Enhanced Caching** via `gradle-actions-caching`, an optimized provider powered by proprietary technology. This feature is **free for all public repositories** and is currently available as a **Free Preview** for private repositories. 
[codex] >
[codex] > **Prefer a 100% Open Source (MIT) path?**
[codex] > We also provide a **Basic Caching** provider as a thin wrapper over `actions/cache`. This provider is **free for all repositories** (public and private) and can be enabled at any time by setting `cache-provider: basic`.
[codex] >
[codex] > For a full breakdown of the components, usage tiers, and our **Safe Harbor** data privacy commitment, see our [Distribution & Licensing Guide](../DISTRIBUTION.md).
[codex] 
[codex] ## General usage
[codex] 
[codex] The following workflow will generate a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submit it immediately to the repository via the
[codex] Dependency Submission API. For most projects, this default configuration should be all that you need.
[codex] 
[codex] Simply add this as a new workflow file to your repository (eg `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml`).
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     branches: [ 'main' ]
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: write
[codex] 
[codex] jobs:
[codex]   dependency-submission:
[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex]     steps:
[codex]     - uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex]     - uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex]       with:
[codex]         distribution: temurin
[codex]         java-version: 17
[codex] 
[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### Gradle execution
[codex] 
[codex] To generate a dependency graph, the `dependency-submission` action must perform a Gradle execution that resolves
[codex] the dependencies of the project. All dependencies that are resolved in this execution will be included in the 
[codex] generated dependency graph.  By default action executes a built-in task that is designed to resolve all build dependencies
[codex] (`:ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies`).
[codex] 
[codex] The action looks for a Gradle project in the root of the workspace, and executes this project with
[codex] the Gradle wrapper, if configured for the project. If the wrapper is not configured, whatever `gradle` available
[codex] on the command-line will be used.
[codex] 
[codex] The action provides the ability to override the Gradle version and task to execute, as well as provide 
[codex] additional arguments that will be passed to Gradle on the command-line. See [Configuration Parameters](#configuration-parameters) below.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Selecting a cache provider
[codex] 
[codex] See [Selecting a cache provider](setup-gradle.md#selecting-a-cache-provider) for details on choosing between the `enhanced` (default) and `basic` cache providers via the `cache-provider` input.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Disabling caching
[codex] 
[codex] Caching is enabled by default. You can disable caching for the action as follows:
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] cache-disabled: true
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### Publishing a Develocity Build Scan® from your dependency submission workflow
[codex] 
[codex] You can automatically publish a free Develocity Build Scan on every run of `gradle/actions/dependency-submission`. 
[codex] Three input parameters are required, one to enable publishing and two more to accept the 
[codex] [Develocity terms of use](https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use).
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex]       with:
[codex]         build-scan-publish: true
[codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-url: "https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use"
[codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-agree: "yes"
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] A Build Scan makes it easy to determine the source of any dependency vulnerabilities in your project.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Configuration parameters
[codex] 
[codex] In some cases, the default action configuration will not be sufficient, and additional action parameters will need to be specified.
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex]     - name: Generate and save dependency graph
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex]       with:
[codex]         # Use a particular Gradle version instead of the configured wrapper.
[codex]         gradle-version: '8.6'
[codex] 
[codex]         # The gradle project is not in the root of the repository.
[codex]         build-root-directory: my-gradle-project
[codex] 
[codex]         # Choose a task that will trigger dependency resolution
[codex]         dependency-resolution-task: myDependencyResolutionTask
[codex] 
[codex]         # Additional arguments that should be passed to execute Gradle
[codex]         additional-arguments: --no-configuration-cache
[codex] 
[codex]         # Enable configuration-cache reuse for this build.
[codex]         cache-encryption-key: ${{ secrets.GRADLE_ENCRYPTION_KEY }}
[codex] 
[codex]         # Do not attempt to submit the dependency-graph. Save it as a workflow artifact.
[codex]         dependency-graph: generate-and-upload
[codex] 
[codex]         # Change the number of days that workflow artifacts are retained. (Default is 30 days).
[codex]         artifact-retention-days: 5
[codex] 
[codex]         # Specify the location where dependency graph files will be generated.
[codex]         dependency-graph-report-dir: custom-report-dir
[codex] 
[codex]         # By default, failure to generate a dependency graph will cause the workflow to fail
[codex]         dependency-graph-continue-on-failure: true
[codex] 
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] See the [Action Metadata file](../dependency-submission/action.yml) for a more detailed description of each input parameter.
[codex] 
[codex] The `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin` can be further 
[codex] [configured via a number of environment variables](https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin?#required-environment-variables). 
[codex] These will be automatically set by the `dependency-submission` action, but you may override these values 
[codex] by setting them explicitly in your workflow file.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Reducing storage costs for saved dependency graph artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] By default, the dependency graph that is generated is stored as a workflow artifact.
[codex] To reduce storage costs for these artifacts, you can:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Set the `artifact-retention-days`:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex]     - name: Generate dependency graph but only store workflow artifacts for 1 day
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex]       with:
[codex]         artifact-retention-days: 1 # Default is 30 days or as configured for repository
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] 2. Disable storing dependency-graph artifacts using `generate-and-submit`
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph but do not store as workflow artifact
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex]       with:
[codex]         dependency-graph: 'generate-and-submit' # Default value is 'generate-submit-and-upload'
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] # Resolving a dependency vulnerability
[codex] 
[codex] ## Finding the source of a dependency vulnerability
[codex] 
[codex] Once you have submitted a dependency graph, you may receive Dependabot Alerts warning about vulnerabilities in
[codex] dependencies of your project. In the case of transitive dependencies, it may not be obvious how that dependency is
[codex] used or what you can do to address the vulnerability alert.
[codex] 
[codex] The first step to investigating a Dependabot Alert is to determine the source of the dependency. One of the best ways to 
[codex] do so is with a free Develocity Build Scan®, which makes it easy to explore the dependencies resolved in your build.
[codex] 
[codex] &lt;img width="1069" alt="image" src="https://github.com/gradle/actions/assets/179734/3a637dfd-396c-4e94-8332-dcc6eb5a35ac">
[codex] 
[codex] In this example, we are searching for dependencies matching the name 'com.squareup.okio:okio' in the _Build Dependencies_ of 
[codex] the project. You can easily see that this dependency originates from 'com.github.ben-manes:gradle-versions-plugin'.
[codex] Knowing the source of the dependency can help determine how to deal with the Dependabot Alert.
[codex] 
[codex] Note that you may need to look at both the _Dependencies_ and the _Build Dependencies_ of your project to find the
[codex] offending dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] ### When you cannot publish a Build Scan®
[codex] 
[codex] If publishing a free Build Scan to https://scans.gradle.com isn't an option, and you don't have access to a private [Develocity
[codex] server](https://gradle.com/) for your project, you can obtain information about the each resolved dependency by running the `dependency-submission` workflow with debug logging enabled.
[codex] 
[codex] The simplest way to do so is to re-run the dependency-submission job with debug logging enabled:
[codex] 
[codex] &lt;img width="665" alt="image" src="https://github.com/gradle/actions/assets/179734/d95b889a-09fb-4731-91f2-baebbf647e31">
[codex] 
[codex] When you do so, the Gradle build that generates the dependency-graph will include a log message for each dependency version included in the graph.
[codex] Given the details in one log message, you can run (locally) the built-in [dependencyInsight](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/viewing_debugging_dependencies.html#dependency_insights) task
[codex] to determine exactly how the dependency was resolved. 
[codex] 
[codex] For example, given the following message in the logs:
[codex] ```
[codex] Detected dependency 'com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre': project = ':my-subproject', configuration = 'compileClasspath'
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] You would run the following command locally:
[codex] ```
[codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:dependencyInsight --configuration compileClasspath --dependency com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] #### Dealing with 'classpath' configuration
[codex] 
[codex] If the configuration value in the log message is "classpath" then instead of running `dependency-insight` you'll need to run the Gradle
[codex] `buildEnvironment` task.
[codex] 
[codex] For example, given the following message in the logs:
[codex] ```
[codex] Detected dependency 'xerces:xercesImpl:2.12.2': project = ':my-subproject', configuration = 'classpath'
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] You would run the following command locally to expose the `xercesImpl` dependency:
[codex] ```
[codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:buildEnvironment | grep -C 5 xercesImpl
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Updating the dependency version
[codex] 
[codex] Once you've discovered the source of the dependency, the most obvious fix is to update the dependency to a patched version that does not
[codex] suffer the vulnerability. For direct dependencies, this is often straightforward.  But for transitive dependencies it can be tricky.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Dependency source is specified directly in the build
[codex] 
[codex] If the dependency is used to compile your code or run your tests, it's normal for the underlying "source" of the dependency to have a
[codex] version configured directly in the build. For example, if you have a vulnerable version of `com.squareup.okio:okio` in your `compileClasspath`, then
[codex] it's likely you have a dependency like `com.squareup.moshi:moshi` configured as an `api` or `implementation` dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] In this case there are 2 possibilities:
[codex] 1. There is a newer, compatible version of `com.squareup.moshi:moshi` available, and you can just bump the version number.
[codex] 2. There isn't a newer, compatible version of `com.squareup.moshi:moshi`
[codex] 
[codex] In the second case, you can add a Dependency Constraint, to force the use of the newest version of `com.squareup.okio`:
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] dependencies {
[codex]   implementation("com.squareup.moshi:moshi:1.12.0")
[codex]   constraints {
[codex]     // Force a newer version of okio in transitive resolution
[codex]     implementation("com.squareup.okio:okio:3.6.0")
[codex]   }
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### Dependency source is a plugin classpath
[codex] 
[codex] If the vulnerable dependency is introduced by a Gradle plugin, again the best option is to look for a newer version of the plugin.
[codex] But if none is available, you can still use a dependency constraint to force a newer transitive version to be used.
[codex] 
[codex] The dependency constraint must be added to the `classpath` configuration of the buildscript that loads the plugin.
[codex] 
[codex] ```kotlin
[codex] buildscript {
[codex]   repositories {
[codex]     gradlePluginPortal()
[codex]   }
[codex]   dependencies {
[codex]     constraints {
[codex]       // Force a newer version of okio in transitive resolution
[codex]       classpath("com.squareup.okio:okio:3.6.0")
[codex]     }
[codex]   }
[codex] }
[codex] plugins {
[codex]   id("com.github.ben-manes.versions") version("0.51.0")
[codex] }
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Limiting the dependencies that appear in the dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] By default, the `dependency-submission` action attempts to detect all dependencies declared and used by your Gradle build.
[codex] At times it may helpful to limit the dependencies reported to GitHub, to avoid security alerts for dependencies that 
[codex] don't form a critical part of your product. For example, a vulnerability in the tool you use to generate documentation 
[codex] may not be as important as a vulnerability in one of your runtime dependencies.
[codex] 
[codex] The `dependency-submission` action provides a convenient mechanism to filter the projects and configurations that
[codex] contribute to the dependency graph.
[codex] 
[codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] > Ideally, all dependencies involved in building and testing a project will be extracted and reported in a dependency graph. 
[codex] > These dependencies would be assigned to different scopes (eg development, runtime, testing) and the GitHub UI would make it easy to opt-in to security alerts for different dependency scopes.
[codex] > However, this functionality does not yet exist.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Selecting Gradle projects that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] If you do not want the dependency graph to include dependencies from every project in your build, 
[codex] you can easily exclude or include certain projects from the dependency extraction process.
[codex] 
[codex] To restrict which Gradle subprojects contribute to the report, specify which projects to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-projects` and `dependency-graph-include-projects` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] 
[codex] Note that excluding a project in this way only removes dependencies that are _resolved_ as part of that project, and may
[codex] not necessarily remove all dependencies _declared_ in that project. If another project depends on the excluded project
[codex] then it may transitively resolve dependencies declared in the excluded project: these dependencies will still be included
[codex] in the generated dependency graph.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Selecting Gradle configurations that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] Similarly to Gradle projects, it is possible to exclude or include a set of dependency configurations from dependency graph generation,
[codex] so that only dependencies resolved by the included configurations are reported.
[codex] 
[codex] To restrict which Gradle configurations contribute to the report, specify which configurations to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-configurations` and `dependency-graph-include-configurations` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] 
[codex] Note that configuration exclusion applies to the configuration in which the dependency is _resolved_ which is not necessarily
[codex] the configuration where the dependency is _declared_. For example if you decare a dependency as `implementation` in
[codex] a Java project, that dependency will be resolved in `compileClasspath`, `runtimeClasspath` and possibly other configurations.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Example of project and configuration filtering
[codex] 
[codex] For example, if you want to exclude dependencies resolved by the `buildSrc` project, and exclude dependencies from the `testCompileClasspath` and `testRuntimeClasspath` configurations, you would use the following configuration:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex]       with:
[codex]         # Exclude all dependencies that originate solely in the 'buildSrc' project
[codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-projects: ':buildSrc'
[codex]         # Exclude dependencies that are only resolved in test classpaths
[codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-configurations: '.*[Tt]est(Compile|Runtime)Classpath'
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] # Advance usage scenarios
[codex] 
[codex] ## Using a custom plugin repository
[codex] 
[codex] By default, the action downloads the `github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin` from the Gradle Plugin Portal (https://plugins.gradle.org). If your GitHub Actions environment does not have access to this URL, you can specify a custom plugin repository to use with an environment variable.
[codex] 
[codex] See [the setup-gradle docs](setup-gradle.md#using-a-custom-plugin-repository) for details.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Integrating the `dependency-review-action`
[codex] 
[codex] The GitHub [dependency-review-action](https://github.com/actions/dependency-review-action) helps you 
[codex] understand dependency changes (and the security impact of these changes) for a pull request,
[codex] by comparing the dependency graph for the pull-request with that of the HEAD commit.
[codex] 
[codex] Integrating the Dependency Review Action requires 2 changes to your workflows:
[codex] 
[codex] #### 1. Add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing Dependency Submission workflow.
[codex] 
[codex] In order to perform Dependency Review on a pull request, the dependency graph must be submitted for the pull request.
[codex] To do this, simply add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing dependency submission workflow.
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     branches: [ 'main' ]
[codex]   pull_request:
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: write
[codex] name: Gradle Dependency Submission
[codex] description: Generates a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submits it via the Dependency Submission API
[codex] 
[codex] inputs:
[codex]   # Gradle execution configuration
[codex]   gradle-version:
[codex]     description: |
[codex]       Gradle version to use. If specified, this Gradle version will be downloaded, added to the PATH and used for invoking Gradle.
[codex]       If not provided, it is assumed that the project uses the Gradle Wrapper.
[codex]     required: false
[codex] 
[codex]   build-root-directory:
[codex]     description: Path to the root directory of the build. Default is the root of the GitHub workspace.
[codex]     required: false
[codex] 
[codex]   dependency-resolution-task:
[codex]     description: |

[codex]     required: false

[codex]     description: |
[codex]       Additional arguments to pass to Gradle when generating the dependency graph.
[codex]       For example, `--no-configuration-cache --stacktrace`.
[codex]     required: false
[codex]

[codex]     description: |

[codex]       'enhanced' (default) uses the full-featured commercial caching service (gradle-actions-caching).
[codex]       'basic' uses a simple open-source caching implementation based on GitHub Actions cache.
[codex]       'external' disables Gradle User Home caching by this action, for use when Gradle User Home is already saved and restored by another mechanism (e.g. Develocity Artifact Cache).
[codex]     required: false
[codex]     default: 'enhanced'
[codex]

[codex]     description: When 'true', all caching is disabled. No entries will be written to or read from the cache.

[codex]     description: |

[codex]     required: false

[codex]     description: |

[codex]       This behaviour can be disabled ('never'), or configured to always run irrespective of the build outcome ('always').
[codex]       Valid values are 'never', 'on-success' and 'always'.
[codex]     required: false

[codex]

[codex]       Valid values are:
[codex]         'generate-and-submit': Generates a dependency graph for the project and submits it in the same Job.
[codex]         'generate-submit-and-upload (default)': As per 'generate-and-submit', but also saves the dependency graph as a workflow artifact.

[codex] # GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin
[codex] 
[codex] A Gradle plugin for generating a GitHub dependency graph for a Gradle build, which can be uploaded to the [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] 
[codex] ## Usage
[codex] This plugin is designed to be used in a GitHub Actions workflow, and is tightly integrated into the 
[codex] [gradle/actions/dependency-submission](https://github.com/gradle/actions/tree/main/dependency-submission) action.
[codex] 
[codex] For other uses, the [core plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin) (`org.gradle.github.GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin`) 
[codex] should be applied to the `Gradle` instance via a Gradle init script as follows:
[codex] 
[codex] ```groovy
[codex] import org.gradle.github.GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] initscript {
[codex]   repositories {
[codex]     maven {
[codex]       url = uri("https://plugins.gradle.org/m2/")
[codex]     }
[codex]   }
[codex]   dependencies {
[codex]     classpath("org.gradle:github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin:+")
[codex]   }
[codex] }
[codex] apply plugin: GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] This causes 2 separate plugins to be applied, that can be used independently:
[codex] - `GitHubDependencyExtractorPlugin` collects all dependencies that are resolved during a build execution and writes these to a file. The output file can be found at `&lt;root>/build/reports/github-depenency-graph-snapshots/&lt;job-correlator>.json`.
[codex] - `ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin` creates a `ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies` task that will attempt to resolve all dependencies for a Gradle build, by simply invoking `dependencies` on all projects.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Environment variables
[codex] 
[codex] The following environment variables configure the snapshot generated by the `GitHubDependencyExtractorPlugin`. See the [GitHub Dependency Submission API docs](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission?apiVersion=2022-11-28) for details:
[codex] 
[codex] #### Required environment variables
[codex] 
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_CORRELATOR`: Sets the `job.correlator` value for the dependency submission
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_ID`: Sets the `job.id` value for the dependency submission
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REF`: Sets the `ref` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_SHA`: Sets the `sha` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_WORKSPACE`: Sets the root directory of the github repository. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] - `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` (optional): Specifies where the dependency graph report will be generated. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] 
[codex] #### Optional environment variables
[codex] 
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_NAME`: Sets the `detector.name` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin`
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_VERSION`: Sets the `detector.version` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to current version of the plugin.
[codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_URL`: Sets the `detector.url` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin`
[codex] 
[codex] Each of these values can also be provided via a system property. 
[codex] eg: Env var `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` can be set with `-DDEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR=...` on the command-line.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Filtering which Gradle Configurations contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] If you do not want to include every dependency configuration in every project in your build, you can limit the
[codex] dependency extraction to a subset of these.
[codex] 
[codex] The following parameters control the set of projects and configurations that contribute dependencies.
[codex] Each of these is a regular expression value, and can set either as an environment variable or as a system property on the command line.
[codex] 
[codex] | Property                                | Description               | Default                         |
[codex] |-----------------------------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_INCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects to include       | All projects are included       |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_EXCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects to exclude       | No projects are excluded        |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_INCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations to include | All configurations are included |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_EXCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations to exclude | No configurations are excluded  |
[codex] 
[codex] ### Controlling the scope of dependencies in the dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] The GitHub dependency graph allows a scope to be assigned to each reported dependency.
[codex] The only permissible values for scope are 'runtime' and 'development'.
[codex] 
[codex] The following parameters control the set of projects and configurations that provide 'runtime' scoped dependencies.
[codex] Any dependency resolution that does not match these parameters will be scoped 'development'.
[codex] 
[codex] Each of these parameters is a regular expression value, and can set either as an environment variable or as a system property on the command line.
[codex] 
[codex] | Property                                        | Description                                               | Default                         |
[codex] |-------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------|
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_INCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects that can provide 'runtime' dependencies          | All projects are included       |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_EXCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects that do not provide 'runtime' dependencies       | No projects are excluded        |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_INCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations that contain 'runtime' dependencies        | All configurations are included |
[codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_EXCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations that do not contain 'runtime' dependencies | No configurations are excluded  |
[codex] 
[codex] By default, no scope is assigned to dependencies in the graph. To enable scopes in the generated dependency graph,
[codex] at least one of these parameters must be configured.
[codex] 
[codex] For dependencies that are resolved in multiple projects and/or multiple configurations, only a single 'runtime' scoped resolution
[codex] is required for that dependency to be scoped 'runtime'.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Gradle compatibility
[codex] 
[codex] The plugin is compatible with most versions of Gradle >= 5.2, and all changes are tested against a range of versions.
[codex] 
[codex] The plugin is compatible with running Gradle with the configuration-cache enabled: this support is
[codex] limited to Gradle "8.1.0" and later. Earlier Gradle versions will not work with `--configuration-cache`.
[codex] Note that no dependency graph will be generated when configuration state is loaded from the configuration-cache.
[codex] 
[codex] | Gradle version | Compatible | Compatible with configuration-cache |
[codex] | -------------- | ------- | ------------------------ |
[codex] | 1.x - 4.x      | :x: | :x: |
[codex] | 5.0 - 5.1.1 | :x: | :x: |
[codex] | 5.2 - 5.6.4 | ✅ | :x: |
[codex] | 6.0 - 6.9.4 | ✅ | :x: |
[codex] | 7.0 - 7.0.2 | :x: | :x: |
[codex] | 7.1 - 7.6.6 | ✅ | :x: |
[codex] | 8.0 - 8.0.2 | ✅ | :x: |
[codex] | 8.1 - 8.14.4 | ✅ | ✅ |
[codex] | 9.0.0 - 9.4.1 | ✅ | ✅ |
[codex] 
[codex] ### Dependency verification
[codex] 
[codex] When using this plugin with [dependency signature verification enabled](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/dependency_verification.html#sec:signature-verification), 
[codex] the you should be able to update your `dependency-verification.xml` file using `--write-verification-metadata pgp,sha256`.
[codex] 
[codex] However, if this doesn't work, you can add the following to your `dependency-verificaton.xml` file:
[codex] 
[codex] ```xml
[codex] &lt;trusted-keys>
[codex]    &lt;trusted-key id="7B79ADD11F8A779FE90FD3D0893A028475557671" group="org.gradle" name="github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin"/>
[codex] &lt;/trusted-keys>
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Using the plugin to generate dependency reports
[codex] 
[codex] As well as the `GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin`, which is tailored for use by the [gradle/actions/dependency-submission](https://github.com/gradle/actions/tree/main/dependency-submission) GitHub Action, this repository also provides the `SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin`, which generates dependency-graph outputs in simple text format.
[codex] 
[codex] To use the `SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin` you'll need to create an `init.gradle` file to apply the plugin to your project:
[codex] 
[codex] ```groovy
[codex] initscript {
[codex]     repositories {
[codex]         gradlePluginPortal()
[codex]     }
[codex]     dependencies {
[codex]         classpath "org.gradle:github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin:+"
[codex]     }
[codex] }
[codex] apply plugin: org.gradle.dependencygraph.simple.SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] and then execute the task to resolve all dependencies in your project:
[codex] 
[codex] ```shell
[codex] ./gradlew -I init.gradle --dependency-verification=off --no-configuration-cache --no-configure-on-demand :ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] You'll find the generated files in `build/reports/dependency-graph-snapshots`.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Using dependency reports to determine the underlying source of a dependency
[codex] 
[codex] After generating the dependency reports as described, it is possible to determine the dependency source by:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Locate the dependency (including matching version) in the `dependency-resolution.json` file.
[codex] 2. Inspect each `resolvedBy` entry for the `path` and `configuration` values. The `scope` value is unimportant in this context.
[codex] 3. Use the built-in [dependencyInsight](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/viewing_debugging_dependencies.html#dependency_insights) task to determine exactly how the dependency was resolved. The `path` indicates the project where the task should be executed, and the `configuration` is an input to the task.
[codex] 
[codex] For example, given the following from the `dependency-resolution.json` report:
[codex] ```json
[codex]   "dependency" : "com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre",
[codex]   "effectiveScope" : "Unknown",
[codex]   "resolvedBy" : [ {
[codex]     "path" : ":my-subproject",
[codex]     "configuration" : "compileClasspath",
[codex]     "scope" : "Unknown"
[codex]   }, ...
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] You would run the command:
[codex] ```shell
[codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:dependencyInsight --configuration compileClasspath --dependency com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] #### Dealing with 'classpath' configuration
[codex] 
[codex] If the configuration value in `dependency-resolution.json` is "classpath", or for some other reason the above instructions do not work,
[codex] it is possible to recostruct the full resolution path using the generated `dependency-graph.json` file.
[codex] 
[codex] Search for the exact dependency version in `dependency-graph.json`, and you'll see an "id" entry for that dependency as well as one or more
[codex] "dependencies" entries. By tracing back through the dependencies you can determine the underlying source of the dependency.
[codex] 
[codex] 
[codex] ## Building/Testing
[codex] 
[codex] To build and test this plugin, run the following task:
[codex] ```shell
[codex] ./gradlew check
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] To self-test this plugin and generate a dependency graph for this repository, run:
[codex] ```shell
[codex] ./plugin-self-test-local
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The generated dependency graph will be submitted to GitHub only if you supply a
[codex] [GitHub API token](https://docs.github.com/en/authentication/keeping-your-account-and-data-secure/creating-a-personal-access-token)
[codex] via the environment variable `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

[codex]     DevelocityConfig,
[codex]     CacheConfig,
[codex]     DependencyGraphConfig,
[codex]     DependencyGraphOption,
[codex]     GradleExecutionConfig,
[codex]     setActionId,
[codex]     WrapperValidationConfig
[codex] } from '../../configuration'
